Select the Arp Options. Tap [Arp]. Tap to select or hold to temporary view the arp options. Button is lit when arp 'type' is on and is unlit when arp is off.

In mono, multitrack, stochastic, matriceal modes the keyboard arp is used and keyboard view will open automatically. Poly and chord mode must be in sequencer grid view.

Quickly tap (Knob) of the respective parameter to toggle between selecting the upper or lower parameter for editing. Tap [Page] button to select between arpeggiator pages. Page button flashes when additional pages are available.

Default values displayed in keyboard arp page 1/2:

| Parameter | Default |
|---|---|
| Type | Up |
| OctV | +0% |
| Gate | 50 |
| Div | 1/16 |
| Accnt | ON |
| Leng | 16 |
| Puls | 16 |
| Rept | 0 |

Page 2/2 defaults:

| Parameter | Default |
|---|---|
| GTim | 0% |
| Grov | Grv 1 |
| Reset | OFF / OFF |
| Latch | — |

### Creative tips for Arpeggios

- Consider layering arps using the 8 sequencers to build complex and generative passages and songs. The 'hold' feature is useful here.
- Modulation and LFO's can be applied to arp parameters opening up a whole new dimension of creativity. Experiment and try things out.
- Remember the flow, probability and logic features can also expand the arp flexibility even more.
- Try changing the reset to differentiate pattern position or groove reset.

### Editing & Controlling the Arp

Turn (Type) knob 1 in arp page 1 to turn the arpeggiator type on or off. Once set to on, quickly change on/off by holding [Shift] + tap (Type), knob 1.

**Hold function.** Hold [Shift] + Tap [Arp] to toggle hold on or off. Hold will retain or remove notes added on the keyboard and not require them to be manually held when played. Hold — button turquoise.

In keyboard view, the [Arp] button will flash if the type is set to 'On' and the arp page is not on display. If arp is on display, button is lit. Arp settings are retained when toggling the arp on and off. Held notes are retained when exiting the keyboard view or when the sequencer is changed. Use in conjunction with 'latch' on page 2 to add and retain multiple notes incrementally.

Turn (Knob) of the respective parameter to adjust its value. Tap the (Knob) to switch between upper and lower parameters. To reset any of the parameters, Hold (Knob) > 2 secs for the respective parameter.

Press [Play] to play sequencer. Play must be on in order for an arp to work irrespective of whether the sequencer is active or not. Press [Rec] to record the arpeggio into the sequencer. The global keyboard arp will also captures velocity and groove. Does not apply to chord arp which cannot be recorded.

#### Arp button states

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| unlit | Type: Off |
| lit | Type: On. Arp page in view. |
| flash (in Keyboard View) | Type: On. Arp page not displayed. |

**Note:** The workflow and parameter controls are designed for easy access and quick, creative control when working in an arpeggiator environment. They may therefore differ slightly to the standard ONE control conventions.

### Arpeggiator Architecture

The ONE arpeggiator is an advanced tool for creating interesting melodies and phrases. The arp can appear complex so taking a little time in this section will help to get a better understanding. The OXI ONE arp consists of two components that work together to generate the output phrase. Firstly is the arpeggiator core engine, this is the component that converts a chord to sequenced notes, assigns the playback order, speed and velocity profile. In addition a Euclidean generator applies further manipulation of the arp generated notes. The Euclidean elements consist of length, pulse and repeats and contribute to the output phrase by adjusting the note sequence and timing to create interesting rhythms.

### Arpeggiator Parameters — Page 1/2

| Parameter | Function | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Type | Off / On — Arpeggio algorithm | When on, sets the arp direction and style for the musical phrase. Also can be set to off. |
| OctV | Octave voicing range | Introduces a number of higher or lower octave notes, into the arpeggio. Range is -7 to +7. |
| Gate | Gate length | Length of the arp note gate between 2–100 based on 'Div' time division. The default is 50. |
| Div | Note timing division | Sets the arp specific note intervals. This can be different to the sequencer. |
| Accnt | Velocity Accent | Varies the arp note velocity in a pattern from a selected 'Grov' groove template selected. Range -100 to +100%. |
| Leng | Euclidean phrase length | Sets the total length of the Euclidean phrase. Range is 1–32, default is 16. |
| Puls | Euclidean phrase pulses | Number of pulses in the Euclidean generator. Range is 1–32, default is 16. |
| Rept | Euclidean pulse repeats | Generates repeats for pulses in the Euclidean generator. Range is 0–16, default is 0. |

### Arpeggio Additional Parameters — Page 2/2

| Parameter | Function | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Gtime | Timing of the groove | Adjusts the velocity timing of the arp notes within the selected groove. |
| Grov | Groove Templates | Selects a groove template. These are applied to the arpeggio timing and velocity. |
| Reset | Reset | Resets the arp pattern position, groove, all or none to start at the beginning when new arp notes are applied. Arp pattern reset on note change is also available in the config > performance settings. |
| Latch | Keyboard Latch notes selection | Works in conjunction with the keyboard hold. Multiple notes can be selected and locked on. |

### Groove Templates (Grov)

| Number | Name |
|---|---|
| Grv 1 | Trancey Groove 16th |
| Grv 2 | Disco Light Swing 16 |
| Grv 3 | Tambourin 16th |
| Grv 4 | Bomba Acc 16th |
| Grv 5 | Shaker ChaCha 16th |
| Grv 6 | Loosely Flow 16th |
| Grv 7 | Claves Accent 16th |
| 8 | Caixa Accent 16th |
| 9 | Charanga Shaker 16th |
| 10 | Dancehall 16th |
| 11 | Disco Indie 16th |
| 12 | Fusion Tight 16 |
| 13 | African Timbales 16 |
| 14 | Quinta Loca 16th |

To apply a standard swing, use Groove 1 and adjust the Gtime parameter accordingly. Groove may apply a velocity profile to the arpeggio or affect note timing or both. User groove profiles can be created externally as well as in the OXI App and loaded to ONE.

### Deconstructing the Arpeggiator

The keyboard arp takes notes from those played, held or latched onto the keyboard. Hold is selected with [Shift] + [Arp]. Held notes are latched, but are released when selecting another note. Notes can be held simultaneously. Latching notes is an option available in the arp page 2/2 and maintains any notes selected incrementally. The sequencer must be playing to select and latch notes on the keyboard.

Arp breaks chord into discrete notes. The arpeggio can extend beyond the current octave. Examples reference scale Chromatic, Root C2, Chord C Major.

- **OctV.** Octave range for playing back the arpeggio.
- **Accnt.** The accent option sets the amount of velocity profile that is applied to the arpeggio. The profile is based on the template selected in the Grov option and creates a soft or emphasised musical groove. This groove is based on velocity deviations through the arpeggio. The reset behavior of the arpeggiator when new arp notes are applied can be set. The Reset parameter is found in page 2 of the arp parameters edited with (Knob 3). The pattern position 'aPos' can be reset only, Groove 'Grve' only or 'All', resetting both groove and arp pattern position. Reset can also be set to off, allowing it to freely run.
- **GTime.** In addition to velocity creating a rhythmic texture, Gtim can also apply a swing style groove to offset the micro timing of some notes. A range of +/-100% controls the amount. This rhythmic style is taken from the selected template chosen from the Grov options.
- **Div.** Divisions for the arpeggio, independent of the sequencer time intervals.
- **Gate.** Percentage of the note gate determines length with respect to the time division.

### Type — Algorithm Behaviors

Type sets the arpeggiator on or off, and if on, selects one of twelve algorithms. The active algorithm is displayed in the arp page header. This generally controls the direction and pattern behavior of the generated arpeggio notes. The arpeggio loops based on notes selected.

| Type | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Up | Notes are played lowest first and incrementally up the scale across the octaves selected then back to the lowest note in the selected range. Example has 3 notes. |
| Down | Notes are played highest first and incrementally down the scale across the octaves selected then back to the highest note in the selected range. Example has 3 notes. |
| Up / Down | Notes are played lowest first and incrementally up the scale across the octaves selected then back down the range. Loops through this cycle continuously. Example has 3 notes. |
| 2 Up / 2 Down | Notes are doubled up at the arp time intervals and play lowest first, then up the scale and the octaves selected then back down the range. Loops this cycle continuously. Example has 3 notes. |
| Altern In | Notes alternative in sequence starting from the upper notes and working downwards in a 'zig-zag' style then back to restart at the upper note. Example has 4 notes. |
| Altern Out | Notes alternative in sequence starting from the lower notes and working upwards in a 'zig-zag' style then back to restart at the lower note. Example has 4 notes. |
| Altern In / Out | Notes alternative in sequence starting from the upper note then lower note before working inwards in a 'zig-zag' style then back to restart at the upper note. Example has 4 notes. |
| Thumb | Notes are generated based on the lower note as the 'anchor' reference, with all other notes alternating in turn with the lower note. Example has 4 notes. |
| Pinky | Notes are generated based on the upper note as the 'anchor' reference, with all other notes alternating in turn with the upper note. Example has 4 notes. |
| Rand Once | Notes are generated randomly throughout the initial arpeggio cycle and then remain in the same pattern for the arpeggio cycles. Example has 4 notes. |
| Random | Notes are generated randomly throughout the arpeggio and change in an evolving, but unpredictable way. Example has 4 notes. |
| Order | Notes are played in the arpeggio in the order that they are played on the keyboard. Example has 3 notes. |

### Euclidean Generator

In addition to the standard arpeggiator function which generates pitch based arpeggios, the ONE arp also has integrated Euclidean rhythm generator which creates more interesting grooves and musical passages. The Euclidean generator operates on the timing of the arp generated notes adding a more complex layer of pattern generation.

A Euclidean pattern is one generated using a mathematical formula for note placement and timing. In simple terms, a number of pulses are generated at equally spaced intervals within a defined length. Think of the ONE arp generating an initial arpeggiated pattern with the Euclidean generator optionally adding more manipulation of note timing and developing the rhythm even further.

#### Euclidean concepts

| Concept | Description |
|---|---|
| Steps | A Euclidean generator needs a time duration, typically beats in the time interval. This can be set in steps to determine the overall length to operate within. |
| Length | Length determines the number of steps in the overall Euclidean phrase. This can be selected from a range of 1–32, default is 16. This is based on the arp step intervals. |
| Pulses | Pulses are the actual triggers within the time period that generate notes. A Euclidean generator takes the selected number of pulses and distributes them as evenly as possible across the time period. The options are between 1–32 to fit the available length range, default 16. The source of pulses comes from the notes in the arpeggio handled in batches set by length. |
| Repeats (Rept) | Selects how many repeats are added to a Euclidean pulse and works in conjunction with the other Euclidean settings. Range 0–16, default 0. |

In essence the actual output of the arp will be the arpeggiated notes further affected by the groove and Euclidean generators. So with length and pulse set to the same number, the output will be a pure arpeggio with no Euclidean affect applied — for example defaults are both length and pulse set to 16.

A good example of repeats in use is with a repeat of 0, length of 16 and 1 pulse: one note will be generated into the 16 step phrase. If repeat is set to 8 it will place 8 additional notes after the original pulse, a total of 9 notes in the arp pattern. This may not be easily recognised where a high number of pulses are already generated or the length is set low.

## Flow

Is flow a tool or a button? Well it's both. The general concept is that flow is a feature that enables specific events and actions to be configured to only operate under a certain condition. That condition being linked to the status of the flow button. For example, notes can be sequenced to play but only while the flow button is pressed, otherwise these notes do not play in the pattern.

Flow can be configured at various points around the OXI ONE feature set. It is not an exclusive function only for note triggers. This makes flow a powerful feature, especially for live performances and improvised sessions. An example is the mono mode accumulator. The Acm% parameter in the step options also has Flow and Flow available. This allows the accumulator to operate, or not, with control from the [Flow] button. Generally speaking, flow is a parameter found within the probability options of a function.

### Flow Step

Events can be set to operate based on the flow button condition. Any 'flow' assigned functions will only operate when the [Flow] button is held. Hold [Flow] + tap [Pad] to add a 'flow' step. Steps will only be triggered in a playing pattern while the [Flow] button is held. Flow can also be applied in various menus including the step menu options.

### Example: Note events set using the step menu

Notes sequenced in a pattern will all normally play when notes are triggered. Trig default is 100%, applied to all notes.

| Trig setting | [Flow] not held | [Flow] held |
|---|---|---|
| Trig 100% (default, all notes) | All notes play | All notes play |
| Trig set to 'Flow' on some steps | Flow notes do not play; other notes play | All notes including flow trig notes play |
| Trig set to 'Not Flow' on some steps | All notes play | Notes excluding the flow set notes play |

Flow configured events are activated when the [Flow] button is held. The 'not flow' option works in the opposite way — flow configured events are not activated when the [Flow] button is held.

Example step menu values:
- D2 — Step 2: Vel 75, Gate 50, Oct +0, Trig 100%
- D2 — Step 2: Vel 75, Gate 50, Oct +0, Trig FLOW

## 5.5 Roller

Roller is a tool for mono, poly, multitrack, matriceal and stochastic modes which triggers a pattern roll at defined note intervals. The roller is located in keyboard view and controlled with column 13 in the grid where each pad triggers a note interval. Arp should be off and notes played on the keyboard with the transport playing.

**Column 13 — Roller time intervals** (lit purple). Each row selects a time division for applying a roll. While holding a note or chord, pressing a roller pad will trigger a series of notes at the interval selected.

| Row | Time Division |
|---|---|
| 1 | 1/4d |
| 2 | 1/4 |
| 3 | 1/16d |
| 4 | 1/8d |
| 5 | 1/16 |
| 6 | 1/8 |
| 7 | 1/32d |
| 8 | 1/32 |

### Editing & Controlling Roller

- Tap [Pad] in column 13 to toggle roller time interval ON — button is brightly lit. When inactive the button is dimly lit white.
- The transport must be playing for roller to be used.
- Play any note using the grid keyboard to play a roller note.
- Hold [Shift] + Tap [Arp] to toggle 'hold' on or off. Hold will retain any notes played simultaneously on the keyboard. Notes are not required to be continuously pressed. Held pads will flash.

## 5.6 Random Perform

OXI ONE can create random variations to existing patterns on the fly, ideal for performing live, improvisation, experimentation or just jamming. Random performance function is non-destructive, varying parameters and patterns temporarily while retaining the original pattern and its data integrity.

Random perform is accessible directly by pressing [Randomizer]. In multitrack and matriceal modes random perform affects each of the tracks independently.

- Quick tap [Randomizer] to open the random perform menu page.
- Hold [Randomizer] to temporarily display the menu, exiting on release.

The principle of the random perform is to introduce random rhythmic and melodic pattern variations while performing live or while playing out existing patterns. Parameters and settings that affect the pattern musically can be 'performed' like an instrument, affecting all steps in a selected track or sequencer. Hold a knob to reset its value — particularly important to restore the original setting.

Select the random generator [Randomizer]. In this mode, switch between the random generator and random perform pages by tapping [Randomizer] or using [Shift] + [Randomizer].

### Random Perform Parameters

| Parameter | Function | Range / Default |
|---|---|---|
| Vel | Random velocity amount applied to the steps velocity | Polar 0–100% or bipolar -100% to +100%. Default 0% |
| Oct | Random octave amount — probability and range for octave variation | Polar 0–100% or bipolar -100% to +100%. Default 0% |
| Gate | Random gate length variation | Polar 0–100% or bipolar -100% to +100% |
| Rept | Repeat randomization | 0–100%. Default 0% |
| Note | Note randomization | Polar 0–100% or bipolar -100% to +100%. Default 0% |
| Trig% | Probability that notes will be played | 0–100%. Default 100% (all notes play, no randomization) |
| Retri | Retrigger randomization (also affects skip) | -100% to +100%. Default 0% |

The behaviour of 'Retri' also affects skips. Skips are added randomly when the 'Retri' value is negative. In the range 0–32% skips and some retriggers are converted to triggers randomly. Values above 33% to 100% increase probability of retriggers being applied and skips have even more chance of being triggered.

### Muting Random Perform

In the Random Perf page, hold [Randomizer] + tap column 1 [Pad] to mute or unmute the randomization.

| State | Color |
|---|---|
| Random Perf Muted | Green Lit |
| Random Perf Unmuted | Yellow Lit |

### Random Perform in Multitrack Mode

In multitrack mode the random perform function operates for each track. The tracks can be muted or unmuted for randomization. This doesn't mute the sequencer, but just mutes the randomization applied.

In the random perf function, hold the [Randomize] button + tap the multitrack grid column 1. Column 1 is a track selection function. Tapping each track while holding the randomize will mute the applied randomization (playing the original sequence) or unmute randomization (applying the random perf variations).

### Randomize Matriceal Matrices

In matriceal mode the random perform option can be used for each track's matrices. This will apply a random series of steps to the selected matrices. The 'Rnd' % is set by holding a step [Pad] + turning (Knob 4); this value will be applied with this function. The randomization can be undone.

- Hold any [Pad] in the upper 16 pad lane section to select and tap [Randomizer].
- Hold any [Pad] in the upper 16 pad lane section to select and tap (Knob 4).
- Hold any [Pad] in the upper 16 pad lane section to select and turn (Knob 4) to adjust the randomization amount. Reflected in the number of steps applied.

The randomization amount set in the parameter page for knob 4 will be applied when using either randomization option.

## 5.7 Performance Grid View

OXI ONE has a unique and powerful performance tool directly accessed using the [Perf] button. The performance view displays a representation of all sequencers on the grid and allows the pads to be used to control the performance of the sequencers while playing. Sequencers can be muted, transposed etc in order to create live and improvised performances.

- Tap [Perf] to open the performer grid.
- Hold [Perf] to temporarily select the grid. Perf will exit on button release.
- In performance view, hold [Shift] to view and access the secondary grid performance options.

### Pad and Grid Zone Functions

| Zone / Function | State / Color |
|---|---|
| Mute / Unmute (Column 1, mono/poly/chord/stoch) | Unmuted = Blue, Muted = Purple |
| Mute / Unmute (Multitrack & Matriceal tracks) | Unmuted = Lit Turquoise, Muted = Dim Turquoise |
| Transpose — original state | White Lit |
| Transpose Down | Red Lit |
| Transpose Up | Blue Lit |
| Rotate — original state | Yellow Lit |
| Rotate Left | Yellow Lit |
| Rotate Right | Purple Lit |
| Multi sequencer (Shift held) — first 8 pads | Brightly lit yellow = Soloed Track |

### Multitrack Sequencer Layout in Perf View

Multitrack sequencers (e.g. Sequencers 5 & 6) are represented with rows as:

| Columns | Function |
|---|---|
| 1–8 | Mutes for Tracks |
| 9–12 | Fills |
| 13–16 | Looped |

Matriceal sequencer (e.g. Sequencer 7) — first 4 pads are mutes. When [Shift] is pressed the multitrack sequencer presents the first 8 pads to solo or unsolo the tracks.

### Muting Sequencers

The first pad column for mono, poly, chord and stochastic sequencer modes will mute or unmute the sequencer. Multitrack and matriceal use the first 8 or 4 columns as track mutes. This centralises control which is important when using mutes as a performance function. For multitrack sequencers, each track can be muted independently — each track is represented by a column 1–8.

Tap a pad to mute / unmute a sequencer or multitrack.

### Transposing a Sequencer

For all sequencer modes except multitrack and matriceal, the central pad group represents transposition of each sequencer. The white lit pad indicates the original setting (no transposition). Each pad to the right increments by 1 semitone and each pad to the left decrements by 1 semitone.

| Direction | Columns |
|---|---|
| Transpose Down | Columns 2–8 |
| Transpose Up | Columns 10–16 |

Tap a pad to transpose the sequencer.

### Rotating a Sequencer

For all sequencer modes except multitrack and matriceal, the sequencer can be shifted right or left in 1 step increments. The original position is shown with the yellow pad column. Access to rotation is by holding [Shift] while editing pads.

| Direction | Columns |
|---|---|
| Rotate Left | Columns 2–8 |
| Rotate Right | Columns 10–16 |

Hold [Shift] + tap a pad to rotate the sequencer.

### Multitrack Fills & Loops

The multitrack has a set of performance view options unique to this sequencer mode. Muting and unmuting is possible for each track individually. In addition there are 4 pads which generate fills of varying intensity, and 4 other pads that capture and replay loops live while the sequencer plays.

Fills repeat notes at musical intervals based on the point of fill selection. Each of the 4 loop pads captures a defined loop step length. Matriceal columns 5–16 operate as random trigger fills.

| Columns | Function | Active Color | Inactive Color |
|---|---|---|---|
| 9–12 | Fills (intensity varies per pad) | Lit Purple | Dim Purple |
| 13–16 | Loops (length varies per pad) | Lit Bright Green | Dim Green |

Loops are initially captured in running sequence at the point that the loop is selected. If changing the loop, the length is adjusted within the existing loop range. Tap a selected loop pad to deselect. Exiting the loop will be based on the 'loop sync release' setting found in Config > Performance options. No Sync, Bar or Beat are the options.

Loop length options correspond to: No Loop, Track Looped — 1 Step, 2 Steps, 3 Steps, 4 Steps.

## 5.8 Snapshots

A snapshot is a tool which captures the state of all of the sequencers at a point in time and temporarily saves the sequencer settings, notes, mutes and modulation. This can then be recalled to restore the previous states of all of the sequencers. Particularly useful in live performance after making improvised or ad-lib changes and song deviations in order to return to the known, original state.

### Capture, Release & Cancel

| Action | Combination |
|---|---|
| Capture Snapshot | Hold [Load] + press [Save] |
| Release Snapshot (recall original state) | Tap [Load] or tap [Undo] |
| Cancel / Clear Active Snapshot | Hold [Shift] and tap [Load] or tap [Undo] |

Performing with the sequencers may continue. Edits and adjustments to parameters can add life and energy to live performances, create drops or breakdowns, or deviate from the routing pattern arrangement. Restoring the original state is then quick and easy. Sequencers can also be saved while a snapshot is active, although the current pattern will not be set to that of the snapshot pattern.

### Arming Mutes in a Snapshot

It is possible to prepare the mute states within an active snapshot prior to releasing. This will allow the continuation of the performance with the original states applied but with the additional benefit of having the individual sequencers or multitrack tracks muted or unmuted on snapshot release.

With a snapshot active, set mutes / unmutes:

- Hold [Mute] + double tap Seq [1] – [8], or
- In a multitrack sequencer, double tap track [Pad] column 1, row 1–8 representing each track.

On snapshot release the original or edited mute states will be applied. These can then be muted or unmuted in the [Perf] performer page. Multitracks can also be changed in the normal main grid, but other mode sequencers will need changing in the performer page as the perf and sequencer mode mutes are handled differently.

### Main Display Indication

The display will indicate when a snapshot is active. The [Load] & [Undo] buttons flash to indicate a snapshot is actively saved.

## 5.9 Other Tools

A few other tools are available in ONE that can be set up and used for more niche applications.

### Monome Grid

OXI ONE can be set to monome view, an 128 grid device. This is configured when powered up so start this process with ONE powered off.

- Hold [Shift] + Power on ONE to enter monome view.
- Restart by turning off and on to reset to normal state.

### MIDI Remote Control

Remote control of ONE from an external DAW or MIDI device is possible. Full details can be found in the dedicated MIDI Remote Control documentation.

# Modes

OXI ONE has eight sequencers, each can be configured with one of six unique sequencer modes. These modes allow a sequencer function to be chosen to match the project needs. For example, a mono mode sequencer is ideal for simple bass lines or lead melodies, also useful to control note-orientated drum machines. A multitrack sequencer offers more flexibility when controlling several related instruments on different channels like complex percussion and automated melodies.

Think of the sequencer modes in two categories:

- **Constructive sequencers** aimed at the building and development of patterns from scratch. These start from a more traditional step by step approach and include Mono, Poly, Chord and Multitrack. They provide the right tools and workflow for defined melodies and beats as well as managing modulation patterns. More organic elements can be added along the journey.
- **Generative sequencers** which use mathematical and logical algorithms to create more free-flowing and organic patterns. These include Stochastic and Matriceal — perfect for exploration and experimentation, but also with the ability to manually edit steps in the process.

In addition, a set of complimentary tools enable expansion of pattern design and move the topics into advanced sequencing territory. Some tools are integrated to the sequencer mode (for example the chord and poly arpeggiators) while others like the harmonizer may be common to several modes. Tools are tagged for the specific sequencer in which they serve.

## 6.1 Sequencer Mode Overview

There are six sequencer operating modes. One mode can be set for each sequencer. Choose a mode that will fit the function and style of the project best. Modes can be easily changed for each of the eight sequencers.

### Summary of Sequencer Modes

| Mode | Description | Complexity |
|---|---|---|
| Mono | Monophonic sequencer. Single track with 1 active note per step. Rows are notes within the selected scale and columns are step intervals. Simple and easy to use. Ideal for quickly creating simple melodic lead and bass line patterns. Useful for transpositions and basic performances. A good place to start when learning ONE. | Constructive sequencer, basic |
| Poly | Polyphonic sequencer with flexibility to create more advanced and complex patterns and arpeggios in a single track. Up to 7 active notes can be applied per step. Rows represent notes. Useful for chords and notes with the freedom to experiment. Patterns can be copied from chord to poly mode. | Constructive sequencer, basic |
| Chord | Predefined chords and voicings in a single track. Grid rows represent degree intervals within the selected scale and can be presented piano roll or sequencer style. Columns are step intervals. Chords can have a maximum of 8 notes. Great for creating chord progressions, pads and arpeggiated loops. | Constructive sequencer, intermediate |
| Multitrack | Powerful operating environment ideal for up to 8 tracks of monophonic melodic or drum tracks. Think of a drum kit being controlled by one sequencer where each track controls a kick, snare, hat etc, with a dedicated drum grid view. Multitrack also includes the Euclidean generator per track. | Constructive sequencer, intermediate |
| Stochastic | Rhythmic and melodic pattern generator. Grid does not serve as a traditional step entry interface but presents a parameter control grid in order to generate patterns. Allows linking to chord mode sequencers. Orientated to probability, randomness and uncertainty — more about experimentation to search and find musical patterns. | Generative sequencer, advanced |
| Matriceal | Built on a matrix of 4 tracks, each with 10 lanes of 16 steps. Notes as well as other parameters can be applied to steps. Core focus is the pattern lane lengths which, when set differently, can generate evolving generative patterns — not just for note playback, but for all 10 parameters available per track. | Generative sequencer, advanced |

### Sequencer Tools per Mode

Functions not listed apply to all modes.

| Tool / Function | Mono | Poly | Chord | Multitrack | Stochastic | Matriceal |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Tracks | 1 | 1 | 1 | 8 | 1 | 4 |
| Notes per step | 1 | 7 | 1 | — | 1 | (per lane) |
| Mod Lanes | 8 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 8 | 8 |
| Performer | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Accumulator | Yes | — | — | — | — | — |
| Harmonizer | Yes | Yes / Source | Source Only | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Arpeggiator | Keyboard | Keyboard & Grid | Keyboard & Grid | Keyboard | Keyboard | Keyboard |
| Roller | Yes | Yes | — | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Generator | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | — | — |
| Random Generator | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | — | — |
| Drum Pattern Generator | — | — | — | Yes | — | — |
| Pick & Place | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | — | — |

## 6.2 Sequencer Setup

Each sequencer has a collection of settings specific to the sequencer or track. The available sequencer menu options will depend on the sequencer mode selected. Each sequencer can be configured to operate in one of six modes. The currently active mode is displayed top left in the sequencer view page.

Access to the sequencer settings is performed using [Shift] + Sequencer [1] – [8].

### Accessing Sequencer Settings

- Hold [Shift] + tap [1] – [8]. The settings menu opens, consisting of multiple pages.
- Tap [Page] to cycle through the pages, or hold [Shift] + tap [1] – [8] again.
- With the sequencer settings page open, tap [1] – [8] to select any sequencer along with its displayed sequencer settings page.
- Parameters are changed with the respective knob. Parameters that need confirmation to make active will be shown with a `*` symbol — press the knob after editing to confirm.

### Selecting Sequencer Mode

- In Page 1, turn (Knob 4) to scroll through the mode options.
- Press (Knob 4) when highlighting the mode type to confirm assignment.
- Mode type options: Mono, Poly, Chord, Multitrack, Stochastic, Matriceal.

For multitrack mode sequencers the individual tracks can be configured. Press column 1 [Pad] to select a multi mode track.

### Sequencer Settings — Page 1

| Parameter | Function | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Ch | Global MIDI channel for the output | Set per sequencer or per track in multi mode. Per track for matriceal mode |
| Out | MIDI output port | Select from ALL, USB, TRS or BLE. Does not affect CV/Gate outputs |
| PC | Program change message | Sent over the selected MIDI channel at pattern load or as configured in the arranger |
| Mode | Sequencer mode | Mono, poly, chord, multi, stochastic, matriceal |

### Sequencer Settings — Page 2

| Parameter | Function | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| In | Punch-in quantization | When stopped, sets countdown for sequencer launch to synchronize sequencers. When playing, triggers the time interval to reset playback |
| Reset | Reset after # bars | Resets the sequencer after a defined number of bars to re-synchronise all tracks. Options: 1–64 bars |
| Bank | Bank change | Sets a bank change CC message to send out on the MIDI channel along with the program change message |
| Trnsp | Transpose Enable | Enables MIDI In to be exclusively used for sequencer note transposition (not for arp etc). Relative to root / base for the sequencer or track |

### Sequencer Settings — Page 3

| Parameter | Function | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| nVoice / T.Swng | Voice count / Track swing | Poly mode: number of voices (notes per step). Multi mode: swing per track |
| Instr | Instrument definition | Assigns a pre-defined set of CC mappings for the selected instrument from the definition library. Can be imported or created from the OXI App |
| Color | Sequencer color | Color scheme selection for sequencer notes, piano roll, keyboard, patterns, and arranger lanes |

## 6.3 Mono Sequencer Mode

Mono (monophonic) mode is the most basic of the ONE sequencers and a useful 'go-to' option for getting started and moving quickly. Mono mode has one track and is ideal for performing, transposing melodies, simple lead lines and controlling automation of parameters.

The grid columns represent a page of up to 16 steps and the rows represent a piano roll style of musical notes within the current scale. A maximum of 128 steps are available across the 8 pages of the grid. Each step can host one note. Rows can be scrolled up or down to access the notes of each octave available in the range.

The pattern will be monophonic, with a maximum of one note per step. Tap one [Pad] button per step column to select or deselect a note.

### Viewing & Editing a Mono Sequencer

| Action | Combination | Behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Activate / deactivate a step | Quickly tap [Pad] | Tapping any pad in a column selects it or deselects if already active. If a step already exists in the column, the new step will replace it |
| Create a tie | Hold 1st [Pad] + tap last [Pad] | Ties all the pads between the two pressed to play as a continuous note. Tap a single pad within the tie to break it |
| Inverted tie | Hold last [Pad] + tap 1st [Pad] | Creates a sequential series of trigger notes between the two pads pressed. Either fills (sequence of the same note) or random note fills, set in Config > Workflow |
| Mute steps | Hold [Mute] + tap [Pad] | Mute or unmute an individual step. Muted steps are lit purple |

Any existing step notes that are not visible in the current pad range are indicated by illumination of the top or bottom row of the grid pads. Scrolling the octave range will bring these notes into view (push or turn the encoder, or use the scroll grid).

### Sequencer Setup Options (Mono)

Accessed by holding [Shift] + [1] – [8]. The [Page] button flashes to signify multiple pages exist; press [Page] to cycle. Hold a knob to reset its parameter. Mono mode has a single track, so these settings operate globally for this sequencer mode.

### Mono Track Menu Options

Press [Track] to access. Tap a knob to switch between upper or lower options in a dual parameter row display.

| Parameter | Knob | Function |
|---|---|---|
| Oct | Knob 1 | Navigate grid view, up or down |
| Dir | Knob 2 | Playback direction |
| Div (random) | Knob 3 | Randomisation of time division |
| Div (rate) | Knob 4 | Sequencer time division |

### Mono Step Parameters

Some step parameters can be edited globally (covering all steps) by pressing [Step], or at an individual step level by holding the step [Pad]. Multiple steps can also be edited collectively. The accumulator is an integral mono mode function (see 6.4).

Individual step submenu (Hold step [Pad], use [Page] to cycle):

| Parameter | Notes |
|---|---|
| Vel | Step velocity (e.g. 75) |
| Gate | Gate length (e.g. 50) |
| Oct | Octave offset (e.g. +0) |
| Trig | Trigger probability (e.g. 100%) |
| Rept | Repeats (e.g. No / 0%) |
| Offst | Offset |
| Durat | Duration |
| Glid | Glide |
| Rng | Range |
| Rpt% | Repeat probability |

### Copying / Pasting Steps (Mono)

The copy / paste options in mono mode allow copying of one or a cluster of steps. Copying mono mode steps also copies their parameter settings. Pasting steps will be applied with the parameter settings and change or set notes relative to their original note position.

- **Copy one or more steps**: Hold [Copy] + tap one or more [Pad]s. Multiple pads can be selected; the step selection count is displayed. Tied steps are copied. The first step selected is the anchor reference.
- **Paste steps**: Hold [Paste] + tap [Pad] for the destination step. This pad becomes the anchor for pasting multiple steps simultaneously. Tied steps are pasted. All notes paste in their relative positions to the first anchor pad.

### Transposing Notes in Mono Mode

Hold the sequencer button and tap any empty pad higher or lower. Direction of transposition and interval is determined by the pad pressed.

- Hold Seq [1] – [8] + tap [Pad] to transpose all notes.
- Tapping an empty pad in a column above or below an existing note transposes all notes relative to the existing column note.
- Tapping an empty pad on an empty column transposes all notes relative to the nearest right-side note.

## 6.4 Mono Mode Accumulator

The accumulator is a tool that triggers a defined step while progressively evolving this step's pitch on each sequencer cycle. This function 'accumulates' controlled note value changes each time the accumulator step triggers. Pitch intervals (typically semitones) are added or subtracted from the previous trigger value for the same step, evolving its note over time. Intervals are based on the scale.

The option to set a probability or logical condition as to whether the accumulator is applied at each step iteration is also possible. The accumulator function is only available for mono mode sequencers.

### Accessing the Accumulator

Hold a step [Pad] + tap [Shift], or tap [Step] button to latch the global view. Hold [Pad] + tap [Page] to cycle through to the accumulator page. Tap (Knob) of the respective parameter to toggle between selecting the upper or lower parameter for editing.

### Accumulator Parameters

| Parameter | Function |
|---|---|
| Amt | Pitch change intervals — defines the pitch interval and +/- direction that the note will change on each trigger |
| Mode | Order of accumulation — sets the direction of travel for the accumulation of notes (Wrap, Clip, Pendulum, plus randomized variants) |
| Acml+ | Accumulation upper +ve count limit before the accumulator resets and restarts |
| Acml- | Accumulation lower -ve count limit before the accumulator resets and restarts |
| Total | Count status (display only) — current position in the accumulation count process |
| Trig | What is affected — accumulation can be applied to only the step, only repeats for the step, or all steps & repeats |
| %Mde | Mode when condition is false — set to ignore or skip; controls accumulator behavior when the logic condition is false |
| Acm% | Probability & accumulation logic — the probability or logic condition that determines if and when accumulator is applied |

### Mode Options

| Mode | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Wrap | Notes accumulate in intervals set by Amt, adding or subtracting to the prior value before reaching Acml+ or Acml-. Continues the count Amt interval while wrapping around the limits |
| Clip | Notes accumulate in intervals set by Amt. Continues until reaching Acml+, then resets and restarts at Acml- (or vice versa when Amt is subtractive) |
| Pendulum | Follows the natural cycle adding or subtracting notes in Amt intervals; changes direction when an upper or lower limit is reached |

An alternative for each option exists which follows a similar pattern cycle but with a level of randomization applied.

### %Mode (Mode when condition is false)

Mode sets the behavior of the accumulation when the accumulator probability logic condition is not met. It evaluates the logic condition and establishes whether to skip the accumulation update but retain and replay the last accumulated note for the sequence cycle, or just ignore any accumulated value until the next update cycle.

- **Ignore**: Accumulation is ignored when the probability condition is not met. No accumulation is applied; the step plays its original value.
- **Skip**: Uses the last accumulated value but doesn't accumulate further if the probability condition is not met.

### Worked Example

Scale: Dorian, Root C2. Settings: Amount +1st, Mode Wrap, Trig All, Acml+ +7st, Acml- -2st.

- **Amount +1st**: increment up 1 interval (e.g. 1 semitone in chromatic scales) per sequence cycle. Range -24st to +24st. If the value were +3st the increment order would be 0, +3, +6, -1, +2, +5, -2, +1 etc.
- **Mode Wrap**: notes increment each cycle and at the max limit will wrap and continue at the intervals set by amount. In this example it wraps from Acml+ through the Acml-.
- **Trig All**: applies accumulation to both steps and repeats. If repeats exist on the accumulation step they are also used in the pitch accumulation count and to the pitch adjustments applied to the repeats.
- **Acml+ +7st**: maximum semitones limit before wrapping or changing direction (if pendulum mode applied). Range 0 to +24st.
- **Acml- -2st**: lower limit semitone position. Range 0 to -24st.

The original note value is the reference from which the pitch values evolve. Only the accumulator step automatically changes its value each time the step is triggered or a step repeat is triggered. The output adds or subtracts intervals (semitones) set by the amount the next time the step is triggered or repeated.

## 6.5 Poly Sequencer Mode

Poly (polyphonic) mode is similar to mono mode but can accommodate up to 7 notes per step. Adding an 8th note will remove the first note added. Poly mode differs from chord mode in that chord mode uses predefined chord formations and voicings while poly allows free-flowing entry of multiple notes per step in any formation.

A maximum of 128 steps are available across the 8 pages of the grid. Rows can be scrolled up or down to access the notes of each octave available in the range — especially useful when using multiple notes on a step.

The pattern will be polyphonic with a maximum of seven notes per step column. Tap one or more [Pad] buttons to select or deselect a note step.

### Viewing & Editing a Poly Sequencer

| Action | Combination | Behavior |
|---|---|---|
| Activate / deactivate a step | Quickly tap [Pad] | Tapping any pad in a column selects or deselects it. Place up to 7 notes per step; the first is replaced if placing an 8th note |
| Create tie | Hold 1st [Pad] + tap last [Pad] | Ties all pads between the two pressed to play as a continuous note. Tap a pad within the tie sequence to break it (useful for chords) |
| Inverted tie | Hold last [Pad] + tap 1st [Pad] | Creates a sequential series of trigger notes between the two pads pressed — fills or random note fills, set in Config > Workflow |
| Mute steps | Hold [Mute] + tap [Pad] | Mute or unmute an individual step. Muted steps are lit purple |

Any existing step notes not visible in the current pad range are indicated in the top or bottom row of the grid. Scroll the octave range to bring these notes into view.

### Sequencer Setup Options (Poly)

Accessed by holding [Shift] + [1] – [8]. The [Page] button flashes when multiple pages exist; press [Page] to cycle. Hold a parameter knob to reset. Poly mode has a single track so these settings operate globally for this sequencer mode.

Key poly-specific setting:

- **nVoice** — Number of voices for the poly sequencer. Maximum and default is 7. With 7, a maximum of 7 notes can be added to each step; with 5, 5 notes; and so forth.

### Poly Track Menu Options

Tap a knob to switch between upper and lower options in a dual parameter row display.

| Parameter | Knob | Function |
|---|---|---|
| Oct | Knob 1 | Navigate grid, up or down |
| Dir | Knob 2 | Playback direction |
| Div (random) | Knob 3 | Randomisation of time division |
| Divis | Knob 4 | Sequencer time division |
| Glide | — | Global pitch change time between consecutive notes. Range 0–100%. Applies to CV only — MIDI doesn't support glide |

### Poly Step Parameters

Some step parameters can be edited globally (press [Step]) or at the individual step level (hold step [Pad]). Multiple steps can also be edited collectively. Step submenu parameters mirror mono mode (Vel, Gate, Oct, Trig, Rept, Offst, Durat, Glide, Rng, Rpt%).

### Transposing Poly Notes & Chords

Hold the sequencer button and tap any other pad higher or lower. Direction of transposition and interval is determined by the pad pressed.

- Quickly tap Seq [1] + [Pad] to transpose all notes.
- Tapping an empty pad in a column with an existing note above or below transposes all notes relative to the lowest column note.
- Tapping any of the last three columns on the right will not transpose as there is no reference note to the right.
- Tapping an empty pad on an empty column transposes all notes relative to the lowest right-side note.

### Copying / Pasting Steps (Poly)

Chord mode sequencers can be copied and pasted into a poly mode sequencer to expand options for editing chords and tweaking progressions. Copying poly mode steps also copies their parameter settings. Pasting steps applies the parameter settings and changes or sets notes relative to their original note position.

- **Copy one or more steps**: Hold [Copy] + tap one or more [Pad]s. Multiple pads can be selected with step count displayed. Copies tied steps. First step selected is the anchor reference.
- **Copy column of steps**: Hold [Copy] + tap an empty [Pad] from the column of notes to copy. Multiple columns with multiple notes can be selected with column count displayed. Copies tied steps. First step selected is the anchor reference.
- **Paste steps**: Hold [Paste] + tap [Pad] for the destination step. The pad becomes the anchor for pasting multiple steps simultaneously. Pastes tied steps.
- **Paste column steps**: Hold [Paste] + tap [Pad] for the destination column. Copied columns will be pasted — a quick way to paste chord progressions.

The first pad selected in a cluster of notes acts as the anchor and forms the reference. All notes paste in their relative positions to the first anchor pad. The column copy/paste process is particularly useful in poly mode when building chord melodies.

### Poly Arpeggiator

Poly mode has a dedicated arpeggiator which uses the notes on the poly grid as the source from which to create an arpeggio (instead of the keyboard). Access the poly arp using the [Arp] button in a poly sequencer in sequencer grid view.

The poly arp is integral to the poly mode engine but follows the general principles and functionality of the keyboard arpeggiator. Ensure a multiple set of notes are programmed into steps on the grid — typically set to longer notes, or tied notes in a chord. The sequencer must be playing. The arpeggiator operates at the defined time division for the duration of the sequenced notes. Individual short notes may not sustain the arpeggiator pattern generation for long enough, so ensure the source notes allow the arpeggio to be generated over the appropriate time period.

#### Accessing the Poly Arp

- Tap [Arp] to select. Hold to temporarily view arp options.
- Grid must be in poly mode and sequencer view. If in keyboard view, only the generic keyboard arp is selected.

#### Poly Arp Parameters — Page 1/2

| Parameter | Function |
|---|---|
| Type | Off / On — Arp algorithm. Sets the arp direction and style for the musical phrase. Can also be set to off |
| OctV | Octave voicing range. Introduces a number of higher or lower octave notes into the arpeggio |
| Accn | Velocity accent. Varies the note velocity based on the 'Grov' groove template |
| Div | Time division. Sets the arp specific time intervals — can be different to the main sequencer |
| Rotate | Euclidean rotation. Rotates the Euclidean pulses + right or - left |
| Len | Euclidean phrase length. Sets the total length of the Euclidean phrase |
| Puls | Euclidean pulses. Number of pulses in the Euclidean generator |
| Rept | Euclidean pulse repeats. Generates repeats for pulses in the Euclidean generator |

#### Poly Arp Parameters — Page 2/2

| Parameter | Function |
|---|---|
| GTim | Timing of the groove. Adjusts the velocity timing of the arp notes within the selected groove |
| Grov | Groove templates. Selects a groove template applied to the velocity profile of the arpeggio |
| Reset | Reset the arp pattern to start at the beginning when new arp notes are applied |

Tap (Knob) of the respective parameter to toggle between upper or lower option for editing. Tap [Page] to cycle through arp pages — the [Page] button flashes when pages are available.

## 6.6 Multitrack Sequencer Mode

So far mono and poly modes operate one track over a piano roll style grid. Multitrack is different — it controls 8 independent, monophonic tracks. The default working view presents each grid row as a representation of each of the tracks, numbered bottom to top 1–8. Each track can have its own individual start and end, length and time division. Multitrack is a versatile and flexible mode, ideal for creating drum kits or developing multi-instrument melodic sequences.

Each track is monophonic with a maximum of one note per step. Sequencer requires 8 voices.

In the default grid view, tracks are structured 1–8 (bottom to top). The first column [Pad] in the 16 × 8 pad grid serves as track selection as well as a note step in a pattern.

### Selecting a Track

- **Select the sequencer**: Quickly tap [Pad] (sequencer pad [1] – [8] for a multitrack mode sequencer).
- **Select a track via column 1**: Tap [Pad] in column 1. Pad will be lit yellow when no trigger is present. First tap of a new row will always select the track first, not the note. Tap the pad again to place a note trigger on the step.
- **Alternate track selection**: Hold [Seq] + tap [Pad] in column 1. Pad will be lit yellow unless a note exists.

The 'track select priority in multitrack' option in Config > Workflow enables tapping any pad to automatically select the track for the pad row chosen. Default is on.

When [Mute] is held: Column 1 functions as track mutes; Column 16 functions as track solo.

### Editing Functions for Each Track

Editing a multitrack sequencer is typically performed track-by-track. Using functions as normal will affect the selected track only.

- **Track parameters**: Many settings can be configured per track — start and end can be adjusted for each track independently. Default mode is to edit the selected track when using functions. Track-orientated parameters include MIDI channel, output port, note offset, time division and playback direction (set per track in the [Track] menu).
- **Step parameters**: Primary parameters like notes, velocity, gate and trig probability can be set per step. Edit by holding [Pad] + turning (Knob) for the parameter to adjust.
- **Global parameters (selected track)**: Adjusting the parameter directly (e.g. turn (Vel) Knob 1) changes all of the parameter values for the selected track only.

Steps can be copied following the normal convention of [Copy] + [Pad]. Steps can also be copied and pasted across tracks. To clear an individual track of all steps, with the track selected, hold [Shift] + tap [Paste].

### Editing Functions for All Tracks

Some functions can be edited for all tracks simultaneously. When changing an applicable setting for all tracks, hold the sequencer button then apply the command for the function.

| Action | Combination |
|---|---|
| Change a parameter for all tracks | Hold [Seq] + [Function] |
| Change end position for all tracks | Hold [Seq 1] + [End] + [Pad] |
| Clear all track steps | Hold [Seq 1] + [Shift] + tap [Paste] |
| Reset all tracks (clear notes, time division, length, offsets etc.) | Hold [Shift] + Hold [Paste] for > 2 seconds (secondary clear function). MIDI Channel remains intact |

Global parameters across all tracks: hold sequencer button [1] – [8] + turn (Knob) for the primary parameter to edit.

## Muting & Soloing Multitracks

The entire sequencer can be muted using [Mute] + sequencer [1] - [8]. Each of the 8 tracks can also be muted individually or soloed. The option to keep mutes in multi mode when loading patterns can be set in the config > performance menu. Randomization and the LFO can be applied to each individual track in a multitrack sequencer. Each track can be activated or deactivated to allow these functions to affect the tracks.

## Track Activation Commands

| Action | Command |
|---|---|
| Mute a track | Hold [Mute] + [Pad] Column 1 |
| LFO Track Control | Hold [LFO] + [Pad] Column 1 |
| Soloing tracks | Hold [Mute] + [Pad] Column 16 |
| Randomize Track Control | Hold [Randomizer] + [Pad] Column 1 |

Tracks are numbered 1-8 bottom to top.

- Hold [Mute] + Column 1 [Pad] of a multitrack sequencer acts as the mute command for each track. While holding mute, unmuted tracks are lit blue, muted tracks unlit.
- Hold [LFO] + Column 1 [Pad] activates or deactivates access to the track by the LFO function. Tracks lit orange are affected by the LFO; green lit tracks are not.
- Hold [Mute] + Column 16 [Pad] acts as the solo command for one or more tracks. While holding mute, soloed tracks are lit yellow.
- Hold [Randomize] + Column 1 [Pad] activates or deactivates access to the track by the randomization function. Tracks lit yellow are affected by the randomizer; green lit tracks are not.

Hold [Mute] + [Pad] on any other column will also mute an individual step note or notes. Muting and soloing the multi-tracks is also possible in performance mode using the first 8 pad columns of the multi-track. Hold [Shift] in performance mode to switch between mute or solo controls.

## Multi Sequencer Parameters

The sequencer setup is accessed by holding [Shift] + [1] - [8]. The header in each page displays the selected sequencer 1-8 and also the selected track T1 - T8. Tap [Pad] in column 1 to select track.

Multi follows the same principles for the sequencer setup, track and step options. Many of the multi mode options apply to each of the eight tracks rather than just an overall single sequencer. The [Page] button will flash to signify that multiple pages exist. Press [Page] to cycle through the available pages.

Multi mode has 8 tracks where specific settings can be configured for each by using the track menu options. The sequencer setup 'T' parameters (e.g. T.Out and Bank) refer to a track specific setting. Several other parameters are global and any changes affect all tracks in the sequencer. Some parameter edits need confirming by pressing the knob.

T.Swg sets the swing value per track between 10-90% or to the 'Same' option which matches the global swing value set with [Shift] + (Swng) Knob 2 in the main sequencer view.

## Track Menu Options

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Ch | MIDI Channel out. Track specific. Knob 1. |
| Ofs | Base note offset for all notes in the track. Knob 2. |
| Div | Randomisation of time division. Knob 3. |
| Dir | Playback direction for the selected track. Knob 4. |
| Out | MIDI output port for the track: USB, TRS, BLE, ALL. |
| T.PC | MIDI Program Change output. |
| T.Swg | Track swing amount or 'Same' to match global setting. |

To reset a parameter back to its default setting, press and hold the respective knob for >2 seconds.

## Setup Page Display Examples

- Page 1/2: Ch 1 A, Div 1/16, Ofs F2, Dir
- Page 2/2: Out ALL, Div OFF, Same, T.PC OFF, T.Swg
- SEQ5 Setup [T:1] 1/3: Ch 5 A, T.PC OFF, T.Out ALL, Mode MULT
- SEQ5 Setup [T:1] 2/3: In Glob, Bnk OFF, Reset OFF, Trnsp OFF
- SEQ5 Setup [T:1] 3/3: T.Swg Same, Instr None, Color 42

## Multitrack Step Options

Some step parameters can be edited per track, covering all steps for the selected track, or can be edited at an individual step level. Multiple steps can also be edited collectively.

## Access

| Scope | Method |
|---|---|
| Track | Press [Step] |
| Individual Step | Hold step [Pad] |

Tap (Knob) for the selected parameter to switch between the upper and lower options in a dual parameter row display.

## Step Submenu Parameters

| Parameter | Default | Range / Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Rept | x 1 | Step repeats |
| Rpt% | 100% | Repeat probability |
| Offst | 0% | Step time offset |
| Rng | No | Range |
| Durat | 0% | Step duration |
| Glid | 0% | Glide |
| Vel | 75 | Velocity |
| Gate | 50 | Gate length |
| Note | D2 | Note pitch |
| Trig | 100% | Trigger probability |

## Special Parameter Notes

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Glide | Global pitch change time between consecutive notes. Range 0-100%. Applies to CV only — MIDI does not support glide. |
| Note | Edit notes of one or more steps while the [Pad] is held and turning (Note) knob 2. The available note options depend on the current scale. Press knob to reset the note value to the base note of the selected track. |

## Alternate Multitrack Views

When all tracks are unarmed, the drum view is the default option when selecting the keyboard. If a track is armed, the view changes to regular keyboard view. The drum view is ideal when working with percussive and drum style instruments, although it can be used with any instrument.

When a track is armed, the grid switches from the default drum view to the regular keyboard representing only the one armed track. Playing or recording with the keyboard will only affect the armed track. Notes are laid out based on the current scale and root.

As well as the 8 track lane view, the pad grid can switch to an alternate view including a drum pad style layout (suitable for drum and percussion instruments) and a keyboard style. The parameter column view editor can also be accessed by tapping the parameter knob in sequencer view.

## Drum View — All Tracks / Unarmed

- Each colored section represents a track
- Pads represent track velocity low to high
- Rows represent the note octave
- 8 track lanes: Track 1 (bottom) through Track 8 (top)

## Regular Keyboard — Single Track / Armed

- Columns represent notes in the scale
- Example shown: chromatic scale with root C, columns C, C#, D, D#, E, F, F#, G, G#, A, A#, B
- Octave rows: C0, C1, C5, C6, C7

## Pad Row Functions

| View | Row Functions |
|---|---|
| Drum View | Roller per track / Solo per track / Mute per track / Arm* (Select) |
| Regular Keyboard | Roller per track / Solo per track / Arm (Select) / Mute per track |

*Track arming not only selects the keyboard view but also affects the MIDI input filtering behavior when set to off in the configuration.

## Piano Roll View and Column Editor View

It is possible to view one track in the classic ONE piano roll view where each row represents a note in the scale and columns represent steps. Access the piano roll view by tapping or holding knob 2 (Oct) in a multitrack mode sequencer view. The currently selected track is displayed.

The column editor is an alternative to using the knobs for primary parameter changes when in the sequencer main view. This lays out the pad grid in columns representing the parameter value for each step for the selected parameter and selected track (for example, to adjust a velocity profile for track 1).

## Grid View — Piano Roll

Example: Chromatic Scale, Root C2.

- Rows laid out piano roll style based on the current scale
- The pattern is monophonic with a maximum of one note per step
- Columns represent steps in time / direction
- Hi Notes / Lo Notes indicators show notes outside the visible range

## Column View per Track

Example: Velocity profile.

- Tap the parameter knob to switch to column editor view
- The columns can be edited representing the velocity, gate and repeat parameter values (e.g. 0-127, 0-100% etc.) across the pattern range

## Controls

| Action | Method |
|---|---|
| Scroll Grid | Turn |
| Adjust all | Turn |
| Piano Roll | Tap |
| Column Editor | Tap |

## Preview Mode

The preview option is useful to audition any sounds played or assigned into the grid in these views. Hold [Shift] + Tap [Keyboard] to select preview mode. The Keyboard button is lit blue in keyboard mode.

## MIDI Input Note Filtering

MIDI filtering setting defines how incoming MIDI messages are routed to specific sequencers, or in the case of a multitrack, specific tracks. Note filtering allows control of incoming MIDI message routing and is a setting found in config > MIDI settings.

While this is a generic option, it needs specific consideration when used with a multitrack sequencer where the 8 tracks have to be managed. Multitrack arm status also affects the filtering behavior, and the filter pays particular attention to root / base notes.

## Filter Behavior Matrix

| MIDI in Channel filtering | No armed tracks | One armed track |
|---|---|---|
| Off | The selected sequencer listens to MIDI notes from any channel. The filter only checks notes. Incoming notes are compared to all tracks' base/root notes and sent to a matching track. If multiple matches, sends the note to the first track. | The selected sequencer listens to any MIDI note from any MIDI channel. All incoming MIDI events, any channel, are routed only to the armed track. |
| MIDI Channel | Each sequencer only listens to notes from the defined MIDI channel for the sequencer. Multitrack filters notes by the track-defined MIDI channel. If tracks have the same channel assigned, notes are routed only to track 1 or the first matching track. | Events are routed to the armed track only from the incoming MIDI channel that matches the track-defined MIDI channel. The base/root note for the track is irrelevant. |
| Ch & Note (Multimode specific) | Multitrack filters based on the track-defined channel and the base/root note. Routes the incoming note to tracks matching the base/root for matching track channels. | Same as above. Events are routed to the armed track only from the incoming MIDI channel that matches the track-defined MIDI channel. The base/root note for the track is irrelevant. |

## Multitrack Generators

Multitrack mode has several integrated generators including the Euclidean style generator, drum pattern generator and random generator. These are useful and creative tools to help build patterns, experiment and explore musical avenues. The interest expands further when adding randomization.

### Euclidean Style Generator

The core concept of a Euclidean generator is to take several parameters and apply a mathematical model to generate a pattern. The two main parameters are the length and the pulses. The Euclidean engine then distributes pulses as evenly as possible across the defined length. Other parameters, for example rotation, can also be applied to enhance the function further.

The multitrack Euclidean generator is a destructive process — the pattern generated overwrites all prior steps unless they are locked or fixed.

## Commands

| Action | Method |
|---|---|
| Generator Menu | Tap [Gen] |
| Select Track | Tap [Pad] - Column 1 |

To change a Euclidean parameter without applying it to the track, hold [Shift] + turn knob for the respective parameter.

## Parameters

| Parameter | Description | Example |
|---|---|---|
| eLen | Length — number of steps in the pattern | 16 |
| eRot | Rotation — pulses rotated left/forwards or right/backwards, wrapping at length start/end | 0st |
| NteR | Note range | 0 |
| OctR | Octave range | 0oct |
| ePuls | Pulses — number distributed as evenly as possible across length | 7 |
| Dens | Density — adds or removes pulses | 30% |
| Nte% | Note percentage | +0% |
| eInv | Invert — when 'yes', inactive pulses become active and vice versa | No |

## Example

Length 16, Pulse 7, Invert Yes, Rotate +2, Density +15%. The 7 pulses are distributed as evenly as possible over the 16 step range.

The generator is applied to the selected multitrack track. Select the track before applying.

### Drum Pattern Generator

The drum pattern generator is an exclusive, non-destructive function in multitrack mode that supports the focus on drum and percussion generative sequencing. The drum pattern generator has 3 engines, each found on tracks 1, 2 and 3, especially aimed at kick, snare and hi hat rhythms.

The X & Y parameters define the source model for generating rhythmic patterns. The Chaos parameter adds randomization. These 3 parameters are common to all engines and apply to all tracks. Density sets the chance of triggers being generated and is set exclusively per track.

The pattern is generated on each cycle of the playing pattern. Existing active steps are retained when using the drum generator.

## Engine Architecture

| Track | Engine |
|---|---|
| Track 1 | Kick engine |
| Track 2 | Snare engine |
| Track 3 | Hat engine |

## Generator Parameters

| Parameter | Function | Scope |
|---|---|---|
| Dens | Trigger step density. Chance of trigger step generation by defined amount. | Set per track |
| X | Rhythm source X. Navigates the 'X' table of patterns to contribute to the rhythm engine. | Set for all tracks |
| Y | Rhythm source Y. Navigates the 'Y' table of patterns to contribute to the rhythm engine. | Set for all tracks |
| Chaos | Randomization. Applies randomization to the generated X-Y pattern. | Set for all tracks |

## Operation

The pattern must be playing for the drum pattern generator to operate.

- Playing Drum Tracks: Press [Play]
- Recommended length: 32 steps. Other lengths are possible but the rhythm generators are optimised to work over a 32 step range.
- Density range: 0-127
- Chaos range: 0-127 (X and Y values 0 to 64+)

### Drum Generator Commands and Indicators

## Commands

| Action | Method |
|---|---|
| Activate Drum Track | Hold [Randomizer] + [Pad] (Column 2) |
| Drum Gen Parameters | Hold seq [1] - [8] + [Pad] (Column 2 blue pad) |

- Hold [Randomizer] + [Pad] on column 2 activates or deactivates the track 1, 2 or 3 drum pattern generator option. The display indicates the state selected.
- Hold Seq [1] - [8] for the multitrack sequencer + tap column 2 blue pad opens the drum generator option menu for the selected track 1, 2 or 3.

## Pad State Indicators (Column 2)

| State | Indicator |
|---|---|
| Active drum generator | Bright blue |
| Inactive drum generator | Soft blue |
| Selected track | Yellow edge |
| Density (Dens) — high chance to trigger | Black |
| Density — medium chance to trigger | Grey |
| Density — lower chance to trigger | White |

Toggle active / inactive with [Randomizer] + [Pad].

## Engine Track Mapping

| Track | Engine |
|---|---|
| Track 1 | Kick |
| Track 2 | Snare |
| Track 3 | Hats |

Drum-generated triggers are populated while the sequencer is playing. Existing step triggers in the multitrack mode sequencer are retained.

## Chord Sequencer Mode

Chord mode enables chords consisting of up to 8 notes to be played and sequenced by each pad. This offers a fast way to build progressions while at the same time learning about chord theory in practice.

## Chord Inversions and Voicing

Voicing is the term used when referring to inversions. Typically the root note of a chord is the lowest bass note. A chord inversion is the adjustment of chord notes to place another note in the lowest bass position instead of the root note. Iterative inversions can be applied. This is also called voicing — the term used often in ONE — and helps make smoother and more musical chord transitions. ONE manages voicing through its parameter application and auto voicing feature in chord mode for the best musical effect coupled with a simple workflow.

## Grid Views

The grid can be presented in two main views:

- **Sequencer view**: chords on each row based on the selected scale and by degrees between the grid rows.
- **Keyboard view**: a multi-functional grid with a piano roll style chord layout and direct mode control using pads.

## Sequencer View Example — Major Scale, Root C2

| Row | Chord |
|---|---|
| 1 | C2 Major |
| 2 | D2 Minor |
| 3 | E2 Minor |
| 4 | F2 Major |
| 5 | G2 Major |
| 6 | A2 Minor |
| 7 | B2 Minor |
| 8 | C3 Major |

Rows represent chords and interval degrees based on the scale. Columns represent steps in time / direction. The pattern is polyphonic, with a maximum of eight notes per step column.

## Step Commands

| Action | Command | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Activate or deactivate step | Quickly tap [Pad] | Tapping any pad in a column selects it, or deselects if already active |
| Create tie | Hold 1st [Pad] + tap last [Pad] | Ties all pads between the two pressed pads to play as a continuous chord. Tap a pad within the tie sequence to break the tie |
| Inverted ties | Hold last [Pad] + tap 1st [Pad] | Creates a sequential series of trigger notes between the two pads. Either fills (sequence of the same chords) or random fills. Set in config > workflow |
| Mute step | Hold [Mute] + tap [Pad] | Muted steps are lit purple. Holding [Mute] + Column 1 mutes the entire sequencer |

## Grid Controls

- Scroll Grid: push / turn
- Octave navigation via knob 1

### Chord Sequencer Parameters

The sequencer setup is accessed by holding [Shift] + [1] - [8]. The generic details of sequencer setup, track and step options have already been covered. Any unique chord mode parameters are described here.

The [Page] button flashes if multiple pages exist. Press [Page] to cycle through available pages. Chord mode has a single track, so these settings operate globally for this sequencer mode. Press the [Track] button to access.

## Track Menu Options

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Oct | Navigate grid up or down. Knob 1. |
| Dir | Playback direction. Knob 2. |
| Div | Randomisation of time division. Knob 3. |
| Div | Sequencer time division. Knob 4. |

To reset a parameter back to its default setting, press and hold the respective knob for >2 seconds.

## Setup Page Display Examples

- SEQ5 Setup 1/3: Ch 5 A, PC OFF, Out ALL, Mode CHRD, Track Menu 0, Div OFF, Dir, Div 1/16
- SEQ5 Setup 2/3: In Glob, Bnk OFF, Reset OFF, Trnsp OFF
- SEQ5 Setup 3/3: Instr None, Color 56

### Chord Step Options

Some step parameters can be edited globally (covering all chord steps), or at an individual step level. Multiple steps can also be edited collectively while the pads are held. The voicing is also accessible at an individual step or global level. Press [Page] to cycle through multiple page options.

## Access

| Scope | Method |
|---|---|
| Global | Press [Step], or quick access via [Shift] + [Chord] or [Page] |
| Individual Step | Hold step [Pad] |

Global control of these parameters is found in the main sequencer 'home' view. Bass affects sequencer and keyboard view. Chords are ordered by complexity top to bottom. Parameter titles refer to both the global and individual step page option labels — for example, Voic in the global menu is the same parameter as Voicing in the step submenu.

Columns 13 & 14 (voicing), column 15 (spread), and column 16 in keyboard view also give access to editing of these parameters.

Tap (Knob) for the selected parameter to switch between the upper and lower options in a dual parameter row display.

## Step Submenu Parameters

| Parameter | Default | Range / Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Rept | x 1 | Step repeats |
| Rpt% | 100% | Repeat probability |
| Offst | 0% | Step time offset |
| Rng | No | Range |
| Durat | 0% | Step duration |
| Strm | 0% | Strum |
| Vel | 75 | Velocity |
| Gate | TIE | Gate |
| Oct | +0 | Octave |
| Trig | 100% | Trigger probability |

## Chord Parameters

| Parameter | Function |
|---|---|
| Strm (Strum) | Sets a slight timing offset on each note to play back in order rather than together. Positive values affect time separation lower to upper notes; negative values affect upper to lower. Range: -100% to 100%. |
| Sprd / S (Spread) | Spread voicing per step. Expands voices of all chords to include a higher octave note of each grade, starting from the lowest note. |
| Type | Type of chord. Sets the triad chord of the corresponding degree as the base. Voicing inversions can be added as variations. |
| Voic / Voicing | Voicing or Inversion. Selects the inversion for the chord. Available options: Open, Open 1st & 2nd, Inversion 1st & 2nd. |
| Bass | Base note. Sets a lower octave note based on the root of each chord. Priority over voicing spread limit. |

## Example Display Values

- Chord Global: Sprd 0, Voic Open, Type C min6, Bass OFF
- Step Submenu: S:0, Type C minor, Voicing No Inver (e.g. E1 Minor)

To reset a parameter back to its default setting, press and hold the respective knob for >2 seconds.

### Transposing Chords and Pick & Place Chords

## Transposing Chords

Hold the sequencer button and tap any other empty pad higher or lower. Direction of transposition and interval are determined by the pad pressed.

| Action | Command |
|---|---|
| Transpose all chords | Hold Seq [1] + Tap [Pad] |

### Examples

- **With existing chord in column**: Tapping an empty pad in a column with an existing chord above or below transposes all notes relative to the chord in the same column.
- **With empty column**: Tapping an empty pad on an empty column transposes all chords up based on the nearest right-side chord as the reference.

## Pick & Place Chords

The last chord selected in keyboard view can be placed in the sequencer grid. This is an iterative process: switching between keyboard view (to audition and select a chord from the library of options) and back to the sequencer view (to place the chord into a pattern and build progressions).

Holding the [Keyboard] button temporarily switches to keyboard view where a chord can be selected. Releasing the [Keyboard] button switches back to sequencer view, and tapping a pad places the chord into the selected grid step.

## Off-grid Note Indicator

Any existing step notes that are not visible in the current pad range will be indicated in the top or bottom row of the grid to indicate that notes exist off the grid in the indicated column. Scrolling the octave range will bring these notes into view.

# Keyboard View

The keyboard view offers a set of tools for playing, composing and performing with chords. Select keyboard view in a chord sequencer by pressing the [Keyboard] button. The last chord played will be remembered and can then be placed into the grid in sequencer view. Also a collection of user chords can be built directly in keyboard view and then can be used for performing and recording a progression.

Each column represents a semitone interval respective to the selected scale. Rows represent octave intervals of the chords (Minor scale illustrated).

**Modifier pads:**

| Pad | Function |
|---|---|
| A | On / Unmute |
| B | Off / Mute |

**Pad colour reference:**

| Colour | Meaning |
|---|---|
| — | Out of Scale |
| — | Adjust to next chord |
| — | In Scale |
| — | Chord |
| — | X12 Slots |

**Keyboard view actions:**

| Action | Control |
|---|---|
| Select or deselect keyboard view | Press [Keyboard] button |
| Play chord | Select keyboard [Pad] |
| Choose chord bank A/B | Tap [Pad] to toggle A or B |
| Select chord type | Tap [Pad] — Column 13 or 14 |
| Select voicing | Tap [Pad] — Column 15 |
| Select spread | Tap [Pad] — Column 16 |
| Auto voicing On/Off | Tap [Pad] |
| Modifier Unmute/Mute | Tap [Pad] — Column 16, top |

**Column functions:**

| Column | Function |
|---|---|
| 13 & 14 | Chord types |
| 15 | Chord voicing |
| 16 | Chord spread / Modifier Mute On/Off / Auto voicing On/Off |

Tapping any pad in a column will trigger the chord. Chord layout is based on the current scale and root. Tapping the pad will switch between the chord banks A (purple) or B (blue). The display will indicate the bank selected. Any pad in column 13 or 14 will select the chord type based on the keyboard played scale and root. Pads on rows 1-7 from column 15 will select the voicing / inversions for a played chord. Pads on rows 1-6 from column 16 will select the voicing spread to add higher octave notes to the played chord. Tap any pad to select auto voicing on/off. On sets chord inversions automatically for smooth chord movement. Tapping the modifier pad will toggle the audible preview for columns 13-16 on or off.

## User Chord Slots

User slots are available in keyboard view which store up to 12 chords, captured from playing the keyboard. The root note, type, spread and voicing / inversion are all saved with the chord in one of the selected chord slots. These can then be played back as a predefined progression or melody and can also be used to record into the sequencer or played with the arpeggiator.

**User chord slot actions:**

| Action | Control | Hold Time |
|---|---|---|
| Assign chord to a slot | Hold chord [Pad] + slot [Pad] | > 2 sec |
| Clear chord slot | Hold any keyboard [Pad] + slot [Pad] | > 4 sec |
| Play chord slot | Tap or hold slot [Pad] | — |

Empty slots are shown unlit. Slots with a stored chord will be lit yellow. A message will display when the slot is assigned or cleared. Playing an empty slot will display a 'Free slot!' message. The last chord played in [Keyboard] view can be placed into a step in sequencer view using the pick and place process.

**Example of assigned slot chords:**

| Slot | Chord |
|---|---|
| 1 | Cmin - No Inv |
| 2 | Fminor - II Open |
| 3 | D#major - I Ivr |
| 4 | Dminor - Open Voic |
| 5 | Fminor - I Invr |
| 6 | Dminor 11 - II Invr |
| 7 | D#mar 11#5 - II Open |
| 8 | Dm add9 - II Open |

## Chord Banks

Chord bank A covers the more typical and simple chord options allowing a quick start in developing progressions. Chord bank B covers more dissonant chords like augmented, diminished and dominant chords. These are more complex and require more knowledge of musical theory to get the best from these options when building progressions. It's safe to say that with less experience of the theory of musical structures, bank A is the better place to get started.

Supported scales are the 7 diatonic scales: Major, Minor, Dorian, Phrygian, Lydian, Mixolydian, Locrian, Pentatonic Major & Minor, Harmonic Minor and Melodic Minor - ascending.

## Chord Arpeggiator

Chord mode has a dedicated arpeggiator which uses the notes from the sequenced chords or chords played in chord keyboard view as the source from which to create an arpeggio instead of the global keyboard. Access to the chord arp is made using the [Arp] button in a chord sequencer.
