# Arranger

The arranger is used to create, manage, and perform songs. A song is a complete production of multiple patterns representing the instruments assigned to sequencers and organized in a musical format. For example, assembling an intro, verse, chorus, and outro type of arrangement is a familiar song structure.

The arranger has two primary uses. The first is creating a whole song sequence from start to finish, letting it play automatically through the patterns you've programmed. It is ideal for exporting a full production and making complete tracks. The other scenario is for live performances, ad-libs, and improvisations where patterns can be set up that loop indefinitely, and control of the progression is handled manually. A middle ground between total manual control and full song mode is another use case. It is worth exploring to see which approach fits your workflow best.

The arranger is here to help structure, organize, and play full song arrangements. It's a tool that lets you stay focused on the music while OXI ONE takes care of the transitions in the background.

It is essential to recognize that a prerequisite to using the arranger is to have a set of patterns saved first. The arranger will draw from a selection of existing patterns to build the composition, so good housekeeping and discipline in saving patterns earlier in the workflow is recommended.

Up to 4 songs are available per project and these can be saved and loaded, or will be saved when saving a project.

## 8.1 Arranger Terminology

The arranger is a tool that manages the complete process of assembling patterns into songs. Some new terminology is introduced and it will speed up your workflow to understand these topics early.

**Song.** A song is a state of an arrangement that can be saved as a complete composition created by assembling saved patterns into a musical structure. The song would therefore cover multiple instrument sections, controlled by each sequencer and laid out over time in relevant sections, for example intro, verse, build up, drop, breakdown etc. 4 songs per project are available.

**Arranger.** The OXI ONE tool used to build, manage and play a collection of patterns into the full arrangement. Often, this is called song mode. The arranger can be considered both a composition and a performance tool.

**Pattern.** Pattern isn't a new term but is an important element of the arranger. A pattern is the core building block for musical passages and phrases. It contains the information that plays the instruments' notes, parameters, timing etc.

**Slot.** A slot is represented in the arranger by a column of pads which manages a specific section of patterns in a complete song composition. Each pad slot controls the pattern playback and transitions for each sequencer and its instrument.

**Clip Launch.** Each pattern can be manually triggered and played in a slot or can be set to play automatically and repeat. The process of playing the patterns within the arranger slots manually is called clip launch. Great for improvisations or jamming sessions.

**Launch Quantization.** Launch quantization applies only when the arranger is 'ON'. It is responsible for managing transitions between slots, ensuring timing and sync are managed.

**Master Lane.** The master lane is a single sequencer lane used as the primary controller for the other lanes. A lane is essentially the musical sections for an individual instrument represented by one of the eight sequencers over the duration of the song.

## 8.2 Songs & Arrangement Structure

Songs are created in the arranger. The arranger is used to build the compositions but also to play back an arranged song. The arranger must be 'ON' to play a song as opposed to playing an individual pattern. Each pattern and slot section can then be manually or automatically controlled with repeat count settings.

### Example: Typical EDM Song Arrangement

The layout maps eight sequencers to instrument lanes, with arrangement slots arranged left-to-right across song sections.

| Sequencer | Instrument Lane |
|---|---|
| Seq 1 | Bass |
| Seq 2 | Drums |
| Seq 3 | Perc |
| Seq 4 | Lead |
| Seq 5 | Pads |
| Seq 6 | Open |
| Seq 7 | Open |
| Seq 8 | Open |

Section-by-section role:

| Song Section | Description |
|---|---|
| Start / Intro 1 | Starts sparse, maybe kick, snare, a few more elements added e.g. bass as intro progresses. Length allows initial DJ 'Mix In' time. |
| Intro 2 | Main 'verse' with melody hints or vocal clues to the full production. |
| Build Up 1 | Leaves space and elements to build up the anticipation or tension. |
| Build Up 2 | Continues building tension. |
| Drop 1 | Full on main track. All elements kick in and the tension built up is released during this section. First drop doesn't overdo things time-wise. |
| Breakdown 1 | Breakdown pause and chance to reset on energy. Thins some elements out to take a time-out breather until the tension starts to rebuild. |
| Bridge | Will give one section for something different. |
| Drop 2 | Full on main track. All elements kick in and the tension built up is released again. Possibly repeated after a bridge for continuity. |
| Breakdown 2 | Second breakdown, again thinning elements out. |
| Drop 3 | Final full-on section which can optionally repeat. |
| Outro 1 / Outro 2 / End | Track breaks down gradually to the end. Elements are removed and a section to allow DJ transition to 'Mix out' is considered, possibly kick only. |

## 8.3 Arranger View

The arranger is a tool for managing songs. This is accessed using the dedicated **[Arranger]** button. Selecting the arranger does not turn the song arranger playback on but only opens the tool. This is a separate step in the process and the arranger is turned 'ON' by holding **[Shift] + [Arranger]**.

The arranger has two pages: Main Page 1 and Editor Page 2. The button will be lit white when the arranger is selected, and will flash when multiple pages exist. Press **[Page]** to navigate pages.

### Slot pad states

| Pad appearance | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Brightly lit, sequencer color | Currently playing |
| Active pad lit by sequencer color | Configured with a pattern |
| Soft white | Blank pad (spacer) |
| Soft blue | Currently empty pad |
| Flashing | Queued to play next |

### Blank vs Empty Pads

There is a difference between a blank slot pad and an empty pad. An empty pad is open and freely available. A blank pad is specifically placed to act as a spacer between patterns and also affects the playback behavior. Blanks are applied automatically when spacing out pattern slot pads.

### Arranger Instrument Lane

- Up to 32 slots per song.
- Any sequencer lane can be assigned as the master lane.
- Playback is left to right.

The display shows status as `ARR: OFF   Song 1   M:OFF   0   1   2`.

### Arranger Grid View

For each sequencer, select a pattern to populate the arrangement grid.

## 8.4 Creating and Editing a Song

The main arranger page is the place to select and structure patterns into full compositions or songs as well as clustering patterns into smaller groups for ad-libs and to use in live performances. A state of an arrangement is called a song. Songs can be saved and loaded individually and up to 4 can be saved with patterns and projects.

To edit a slot in the arranger, hold a pad to change the pattern, repeats, program change and bank settings. Press **[Arranger]** to set up the generic options and hold **[Pad]** for a pattern slot / instrument lane to edit the specific parameters.

The currently loaded song will be the one edited when working in the arranger. This is shown in the header of the display. Songs cannot be renamed. Saving songs will save the project.

While the global arrangement settings are available in arranger view, holding a slot pad will also give access to its pattern-specific options. These are set specifically for each pattern slot when building the full song arrangement. Best practice is to set the slot settings before constructing the arrangement. For example, set repeats as the default which will then be applied when adding patterns to the track arrangement.

### Song operations

| Action | Steps |
|---|---|
| Save song | In the arranger, tap (Save) Knob 2, then turn Knob 2 to select a song slot. Press (Save) Knob 2 again to save the song to the selected slot. |
| Load song | In the arranger, tap (Load) Knob 1, then turn Knob 1 to select a song slot to load. Press (Load) Knob 1 again to load. |
| Clear song | In the arranger, hold **[Shift] + [Paste]** to clear the current song to a blank state. Undo is available. |

### Slot / Pattern parameters

Display layout for a held pad:

```
Slot 1 "Pattern 1"
PrCh   Next   -
Bank   Patt   Rept
-      OFF    1      OFF
```

| Field | Function |
|---|---|
| PrCh — Program Change | Options to trigger a MIDI PC message output. |
| Next | Instructs which slot to play next in the song sequence. |
| Bank — Bank Change | Options to trigger a MIDI Bank message output. |
| Patt — Pattern | Selects the pattern for the slot to play and edit. |
| Rept — Repeats | Number of pattern cycles to play before progressing. |

### Constructing a Song with Patterns

There are several ways to add patterns to a song. The approach that is used will depend on your personal preference and workflow. It is of course a prerequisite that patterns have already been created.

Each row represents a sequencer arrangement lane 1-8. Each column represents the 1-8 pattern slots for each sequencer. Hold an arrangement slot to view the pattern assigned.

| Method | Steps |
|---|---|
| Change a pattern by slot number | Select a pattern by its pattern slot number from the current project and sequencer pattern list. Current Slot and Pattern is displayed in the arranger slot display. |
| Change a pattern by grid pattern | In the arranger view, open the pattern grid selection page by pressing **[Page]**. The current pattern will be lit red. Press any pad in the selected sequencer lane to choose a pattern for the selected arranger slot. |
| Assign patterns by grid pattern | To open the pattern grid, hold the arranger **[Pad] + tap [Page]**. Press any pad to add it to the arranger. Patterns are added to the arranger in the order selected from its sequencer lane. Tap a pattern several times to continuously add. Multiple patterns can be iteratively selected. |
| Create a slot column | In the arranger view, create an entire slot column by holding **[Arranger] + Tap any column [Pad]**. The slots are created using the current default settings (so set repeats prior to creating a slot column). Muted sequencers will create empty slots. |

### Repeat — configuring how slots play

Once patterns have been placed into the slots for each sequencer lane, their individual behavior should be set to determine how each pattern will play.

- Repeat sets how the pattern plays before it moves to the next pattern slot.
- Setting repeat to 'Infinite' will constantly repeat without progressing (symbolised by the infinity icon).
- Setting to 'Off' marks the pattern as empty and the slot will be skipped when playing the arrangement.
- The number of playback cycles can be set between 1-19.
- This can be set for slots with a pattern or slots with no pattern assigned (which then become blank slots and are lit white).
- Blank slots will still be activated for 1 bar of 16-step duration (1/16) and the set number of repeats but without playing actual pattern notes or modulation.
- The difference between blank slots and empty slots: empty slots are skipped, while blank slots are useful to hold the arranger position playback silently at the selected slot for a defined period or infinitely until manually progressed.

| Operation | Action |
|---|---|
| Toggle repeat between 1 and Infinite | Hold **[Pad] + Tap (Rept) Knob 4** |
| Select specific repeat option | Hold **[Pad] + Turn (Rept) Knob 4** |

### Slot parameter reference

| Field | Function | Range / Options |
|---|---|---|
| PrCh — Program Change | Sends a MIDI PC message 2 tics before the pattern transition. | See PC settings |
| Bank — Bank Change | Sends a MIDI CC#0 or CC#32 message 2 tics before the pattern transition. | See Bank settings |
| Patt — Pattern | Pattern number for the selected sequencer slot to launch. |  |
| Rept — Repeat | Number of repeat cycles before progressing, or play infinitely. | 1-19, Infinite, Off |
| Next — Pattern Transition | Selects the next slot to play. | Next, Previous, 1st, Random, Other, etc. |

Rept behaviors:

| Setting | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Infinite | Plays indefinitely with no repeat countdown. Progression is performed by manually tapping another slot pad. Can be used for blank slots which are lit white. |
| 1-19 | Plays and counts down the number of repeats. Can be progressed earlier by manually tapping another slot pad. Can be used with blank slots, lit white. |
| Off | Sets the pad slot to 'empty' meaning that arrangement playback will skip this step in the song progression. |

### Next — pattern transition behaviors

Next sets the transition behavior of the slot and determines which slot is played next in the song sequence. Once the pattern and its repeats are completed the song will progress to the next slot.

Hold **[Pad] + Tap (Next) Knob 1** to toggle between PrCh and Next options. With 'Next' selected, turn **(Next) Knob 1**. Hold knob to reset.

| Option | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Next Slot | During the slot's last repeat cycle, the next slot will flash signifying that it is cued. When the repeats and pattern are complete, the next slot available in the song sequence will play next. |
| Previous Slot | During the slot's last repeat cycle, the prior slot will flash signifying that it is cued. When the repeats and pattern are complete, the previous slot in the song sequence will play next. |
| 1st (First Slot) | During the slot's last repeat cycle, the first slot will flash signifying that it is cued. When the repeats and pattern are complete, the first slot in the song sequence will play next. |
| Random Slot | During the slot's last repeat cycle, a random slot will flash signifying that it is cued next. When the repeats and pattern are complete, the cued slot will play next. Randomly plays any pattern. |
| OTHR (Other Slot) | During the slot's last repeat cycle, a random slot will flash signifying that it is cued next. When the repeats and pattern are complete, the cued slot will play next. Does not repeat its own pattern. |

## 8.5 Playing and Controlling a Song

Press **[Arranger]** to open the arranger options. However to play a song arrangement, the arranger must be turned on / armed. The arranger will automatically disarm when stopped. Saved patterns are loaded when playing songs. As such it is important to save any edited patterns to avoid loss.

### Arming and playing

| Action | Method |
|---|---|
| Arranger on or off | **[Shift] + [Arranger]** — arranger button is lit blue when armed. |
| Play the song | Tap **[Play]** — the arranger must be On/Armed in order to play a song as opposed to only the patterns. |
| Stop the song | Tap **[Stop]** — the arranger will automatically turn to Off/Disarmed. |

The arranger will automatically turn off and reset to unarmed when stopped.

### Arranger Main Page 1 — display elements

Display header example: `ARR: OFF   Song 1   M:OFF   0   1   2`

| Element | Meaning |
|---|---|
| ARR: OFF / IDLE / etc. | Arranger arm state |
| Song 1 | Currently loaded song being edited |
| M:OFF / M:S8 etc. | Master lane assignment ('M' indicates the assigned lane) |
| Knob 1 (Load) | Load song option |
| Knob 2 (Save) | Save song option |
| Knob 3 (M:OFF) | Master lane assignment |
| Knob 4 | Navigate grid view |
| Lanes 1-8 | Playback status for each sequencer |

### Page Lane Symbols

Lanes are represented by sequencer rows. The display orders lanes 1, 5, 2, 6, 3, 7, 4, 8.

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Infinity | Current slot is operating in an infinite playback cycle. Patterns in an infinite cycle will continue to play indefinitely unless another slot is manually selected. |
| Repeat-cycle countdown number | Patterns set on a repeat cycle will automatically progress to the next slot in the song. They can also be manually transitioned. |
| Flashing pad | Transition to the next cued pattern imminent. Currently playing the last repeat cycle for the slot. |
| 'M' | Indicates the lane is assigned as the master lane. |

### Clip Launch (manual playback)

Pattern playback behavior can be controlled individually. Each pad represents a pattern for the arranger instrument lane assigned to play, or can be set up to play a set number of times before progressing, infinitely or not at all. Patterns are played in the slot columns left to right or as manually selected.

| Action | Method |
|---|---|
| Play slot | While playing, tap the **[Pad]** for the slot and instrument lane to play. Playback will start based on the launch quantization. |
| Play slot column | While playing, hold **[Shift] + tap the [Pad]** to cue and play the full column. |

Playing slot pattern is brightly lit. Non-playing slots are lit in normal lane color. Next pattern pad or manually selected slot pad flashes color/white to signify it is cued pending playback, then is lit brightly once play starts.

### Arranger Launch Quantization

The ONE arranger manages the synchronization and transition of patterns throughout the song in the background. The function that manages this is **arranger launch quantization**, and the arranger must be set to On/Armed for it to be active.

The arranger launch quantization setting is found in the 'Project' configuration, accessed with **[Shift] + [Perf]**.

Options: P.End (Pattern End), 1/2 Bar, 1 Bar, 2 Bars, 4 Bars, 8 Bars, 16 Bars, 32 Bars.

Launch quantization sets the number of bars, based on the internal clock, to complete one pattern repetition cycle. The launch quantization is displayed as a progress bar on the arrangement main page. This function controls the transition timing between patterns in a song.

Note: an alternate version exists called global or load quantization, which is applied when the arranger is off for things such as pattern loading behavior.

## 8.6 Master Lane & Clip Launch

A master lane is available for each song and can be assigned to any one of the sequencer lanes. A master lane operates as the primary controller for all other lanes. The master will dictate the progression of all slots, ignoring repetition and pattern length settings of other lanes. For example, selecting a slot to play on the master lane will also select the equivalent slots on other lanes.

Playback of the song slots will be synchronised to the master track. Repeat settings and pattern lengths are ignored. Only the master lane controls the progression.

### Assigning the master lane

Turn **Knob 3 (M:OFF)** to select a master lane. Double tap to reset its value to OFF.

| Option | Result |
|---|---|
| Off | No master lane |
| Lanes 1-8 | The selected sequencer becomes the master lane (e.g. M:S8 indicates lane 8). An 'M' symbol indicates the selected lane on the display. |

### Clip Launch & Pattern Override

Tap any arrangement slot **[Pad]** to launch pattern playback manually. Transition is governed by the launch quantization. Tapping a slot pad on the master lane will trigger its playback and the equivalent slot on all other lanes.

While the arranger is on and a song is playing, hold **[Load] + [Pad]** for the pattern to change.

## 8.7 Program Change & Bank Change

As well as controlling pattern playback, each slot also has the option of being set to trigger a MIDI Program Change (PC) message. Program changes are used to change presets, patches or sounds in a destination device. Most presets are organised in banks. A bank change message can also be configured for each slot. The MIDI standard protocol uses Control Change CC#0 (MSB) or CC#32 (LSB) to initiate a bank change.

Program Change and Bank options are also available for each sequencer pattern. Default is '=' to use the pattern settings. These are set with **[Shift] + Seq [1]-[8]** and the PC and Bank options found in settings pages 1 and 2 respectively.

### PrCh field options

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| = | Use pattern PC value |
| Off | No PC transmitted |
| 1-128 | PC message value |

### Bank field options

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| = | Use pattern bank value |
| Off | No bank transmitted |
| 1-128 L or M | PC LSB or MSB message |

The slot will trigger a PC and Bank message 2 clock tics before activating the next pattern. The pattern will play the new preset or sound activated by the program and bank change. The MIDI Channel should be set for the sequencer. Blank slots can also send PC and Bank changes without playing a pattern.

### Incoming Program Change

The pattern column slots, displayed on the grid in page 2 of the arranger, can be selected using incoming MIDI PC messages. The columns are numbered from left to right so the first column is selected with PC 1, the second column with PC 2, and so forth.

Prerequisite: set the MIDI Channel used for incoming program change in **Config > MIDI > PC (Program Change) receive global Channel**.

**Note:** It may need some experimentation and testing with the external device in order to match the program change and bank change messaging. Some preset structures start at 0, some at 1, and some may not count to 128. Try variations in order to find the right alignment between ONE and the device.

## 8.8 Editing Slots

The pads on the grid represent the patterns in the song slot. Each slot can be shifted left or right on the grid, copied, pasted and cleared. The sequencer lane color matches the sequencer color and can be changed in the main sequencer view.

| Action | Shortcut |
|---|---|
| Copy slot | Hold **[Copy] + [Pad]** to copy the pattern slot. Only individual pattern slots can be copied. |
| Paste slot | Hold **[Paste] + [Pad]** to paste the previously copied pattern slot into the same lane. |
| Shift slot right | Hold **[Pad] + Page [64]** to move the pattern right. Also moves or rearranges blanks and empty slots. |
| Shift slot left | Hold **[Pad] + Page [16]** to move the pattern left. Also moves or rearranges blanks and empty slots. |
| Clear song | Hold **[Shift] + [Paste]** to clear the entire song. |

# Connectivity

The connectivity options for OXI ONE are what makes it the perfect device as a centre piece in the studio, for a live rig, or anywhere that inspiration strikes.

- ONE has 8 CV inputs and 8 Gate outputs which can be easily configured to connect with analog gear such as Eurorack modules or desktop devices. It is equally comfortable controlling monophonic modules and polyphonic voices, or simply modulating an external control. The CV and Gates can be configured or set on/off on the fly, allowing the grid to act as a performance interface.
- An analog clock, reset and 2 CV inputs are also available.
- MIDI inputs and outputs are supported including CC, but **not NRPN**. CC mappings are shown in the LFO Modulation section. This can be set up using the TRS connectors directly with a stereo cable or the TRS to MIDI DIN Type-A adapter.
- MIDI is also available over the USB-C connection as well as the BLE (Bluetooth Low-Energy) wireless interface.
- The MIDI ports can be expanded further using OXI Split.
- The OXI Pipe interface connects seamlessly to a Eurorack system using the HDMI pipe connection.

This creates a powerful and flexible sequencer ecosystem, bringing together many devices and creating almost infinite configurations.

## 9.1 CV & Gate Output Configuration

OXI ONE has 8 CV and 8 Gate outputs which can be configured for each of the sequencers. The CV output range is limited by the hardware to **-3V to 5V**. The CV/Gate configuration is set up using the grid pad configurator, and as this configuration covers all sequencers it is saved with the project.

### Rear panel layout

Left to right (rear view):

| Block | Connectors |
|---|---|
| MIDI Out / Clock Out / Pipe |  |
| MIDI In / Clock In / CV In |  |
| CV Outputs 1-8 | Cv 1, Cv 2, Cv 3, Cv 4, Cv 5, Cv 6, Cv 7, Cv 8 |
| Gate Outputs 1-8 | Gate 1, Gate 2, Gate 3, Gate 4, Gate 5, Gate 6, Gate 7, Gate 8 |

### Grid configurator

Open the CV/Gate configuration menu by holding **[Shift] + press [Gen]**.

In the CV/Gate grid:
- Rows represent all sequencers (1-8 from top to bottom).
- Columns 1-8 represent CV outputs 1-8.
- Columns 9-16 represent Gate outputs 1-8.
- Tap a pad to activate or deactivate the CV or Gate output for sequencers 1-8. The display will indicate current setting.
- Blue pads = active pitch type CV outputs; unlit pads = inactive.
- While playing a pattern, the pad columns representing the outputs will indicate any real-time activity.

### Configuring a CV / Gate output (procedure)

1. Open the CV/Gate configuration. Hold **[Shift] + tap [Gen]**. The grid represents all sequencers by row and the CV and Gate outputs in the columns.
2. Tap a pad to select the output, indicated by the lit pad. Tapping the pad links the output from the sequencer to the selected CV or Gate output.
3. Hold a pad to configure the CV or Gate output behaviour. The settings will be displayed, labelled for the selected CV or Gate and the sequencer.
4. Adjust the parameters using the knobs for each function.
5. Also consider the **CV/Gate Voicing** allocation settings found in the **Config > Analog** options. Sets the behavior of how the outputs are allocated polyphonically.
6. Save the project in order to save the configuration. Quick save by holding **[Shift] + press [Save]**.

### Display layouts

CV settings (tapping a 1-8 pad):
```
CV 1 - SEQ 1
Type    Voice
Pitch   1       0 st    V/Oct
                Offst   Format
```

Gate settings (tapping a 9-16 pad):
```
GATE 1 - SEQ 1
Type    Voice
Trig    1       -       VTrig
                Offst   Format
```

Selecting a pad will turn the output for the sequencer on or off. The output behaviour will also need to be set up. This starts by setting **Type** for the CV output, which will also determine what additional parameters are available.

### CV Output Types

Type refers to the type of output that the CV will be used for. Type is selected with **Knob 1**.

| Type | Function |
|---|---|
| Pitch | Note pitch (V/Oct, Hz/V or 1.2V models) |
| Velocity | Step velocity value as CV |
| Trig Envelope (T.Env) | ADSR-style envelope CV |
| LFO 1, LFO 2 | Routed LFO source as CV |
| MOD 1, MOD 2, MOD 3, MOD 4 | Routed Mod Lane source as CV |

The configuration of each will offer a set of parameters exclusive to the type selected.

### Voices

Think of a voice as representing a track or note output from a sequencer. It is the parameter that controls how polyphonic notes are played alone or together. For analog outputs, each voice will require an allocated physical CV / Gate output. So a triad chord will require 3 x CV outputs. But chords are not only triads and more voices may be needed.

| Sequencer Mode | Voice Capacity |
|---|---|
| Mono Mode | 1 voice |
| Poly Mode | up to 7 voices |
| Chord Mode | up to 8 voices |
| Multitrack Mode | up to 8 voices — 1 per track |
| Stochastic | 1 voice |
| Matriceal | up to 4 voices — 1 per track |

### Voice Allocation Rules — Config > Analog > 'CV & Gate voice allocation'

OXI ONE also has to take care of the behavior of the voice allocation under various scenarios. For example, how are voices allocated if four notes are played when only 3 x CV/Gates are assigned? Which notes take precedence when changing chords? Which notes to steal to re-allocate a new note? The rules applied for voice allocation are defined in the Config menu, under the Analog sub-menu.

| Option | Notes Stolen | Description |
|---|---|---|
| LRU | Least Recently Used | If a new note is played while others in a chord are still active, the output voices needed may exceed the available CV/Gate voices allocated. With LRU, the oldest note will be cancelled (also called 'stolen') and replaced with the newest note played. This behavior continues as notes are added to existing chords. |
| LOW | Lowest note | If a new note is played while others in a chord are still active, the output voices needed may exceed the available CV/Gate voices allocated. With LOW, the lowest active note value will be cancelled or 'stolen' and replaced with the newest note played. This behavior continues as notes are added to existing chords. |
| Unison | Sort by Last played | All available CV and Gate voices configured for the sequencer play in unison for single notes. Further notes are divided among the voices to maximize the use of the assigned CVs and Gates. If there are more notes than the available CVs or Gates, the voices will still be sorted by the last played. |

### Pitch (CV Type)

Sets the CV output to control pitch of an external device. The output requires the voltage to be calibrated to the pitch. This ensures that the sequenced notes in ONE play on the external device as the correct note. The calibration varies depending on the device being controlled.

### Pitch Settings

Display:
```
CV 1 - SEQ 1
Type    Voice
Pitch   1       0 st    V/Oct
                Offst   Format
```

| Field | Function |
|---|---|
| Type — Pitch | Pitch is the configured output to control as defined by the available parameter settings. |
| Voice — Voice Index | Voice index based on sequencer mode. For example, Mono has 1 voice, Chord up to 8. |
| Offst — Note Offset | Adjust the pitch by applying an offset in semitones. Range is -48 to +48 semitones. |
| Format — Voltage Format | Set to match the receiving device: Eurorack V/Oct, Korg Hz/Volt, Buchla 1.2V. |

### Pitch tracking models

| Model | Calibration | Typical devices |
|---|---|---|
| V/Oct (1 Volt per Octave) | OXI ONE outputs are calibrated to -3V to +5V across C-1 to C7 (re-calibration is possible but not recommended for normal operation, as ONE is already pre-calibrated). | Modular, semi-modular, Eurorack, Moog devices |
| Hz/Volt | Linear calibration, output calibrated at 0V to +5V. | Korg, Yamaha |
| 1.2 Volt pitch tracking | Designed to cover an even 0.1V semitone representation in a Western 12-note scale. Output is calibrated at 0V to +5V. | Buchla systems |

Note: the V/Oct diagram in the manual is simplified — the frequency perception of pitch is logarithmic in reality.

### Velocity (CV Type)

The velocity value of the steps can be transmitted out through CV. This is a directly accessible feature when working with the CV/Gate options. Useful for adding expression or modulation to an analog device, for example controlling note attack, release or decay.

Configuring a CV output for velocity is similar to setting up pitch. The grid is used to assign outputs to sequencers in CV/Gate view. Each velocity output requires 1 voice and hence 1 CV output.

### Velocity Settings

Display:
```
CV 1 - SEQ 1
Type    Voice
Vel     1       OFF     -
                Gated   -
```

| Field | Function |
|---|---|
| Type — Velocity | Velocity is the output to control as defined by the available parameter settings. |
| Voice — Voice Index | Voice index based on sequencer mode. Velocity needs 1 voice. |
| Gated — Velocity On/Off | Retains velocity between any played notes, or resets to zero each time a note ends. |

### Gated behavior

| Setting | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Gated OFF (default) | The velocity CV output will be retained at the value set by the last note. Any change to the velocity value will only be triggered when a new note plus velocity value is recognised. |
| Gated ON | Velocity will be output only for the duration of the individual or tied note. When the note ends, the velocity will be set to 0. Any new notes will update the velocity. |

### T.Env (CV Type — Trigger Envelope)

The trigger envelope acts as an ADSR (Attack / Decay / Sustain / Release) style CV output envelope. The attack and sustain can be edited. Useful for adding modulation or control to an analog device, for example controlling a voltage controlled amplifier, low pass gate or filter cutoff.

The envelope is triggered by a step note and its amplitude is proportional to the velocity, meaning a note velocity of 127 will output the maximum 5V.

Configuring a CV output for T.Env is similar to setting up velocity. The grid is used to assign outputs to sequencers in CV/Gate view. Each envelope output requires 1 voice and hence 1 CV output.

### Envelope stages

| Stage | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Attack — time | Time to reach maximum. Set as a percentage. |
| Decay — time | Determined automatically from the gate length for the triggering step. |
| Sustain — level | Level held while the note is active. Set as a % of the amplitude (0-100%). |
| Release — time | Automatically derived from the gate length for the triggering step. When a note is tied, the release value is based on the last note triggered by a tie. |

Attack time can be set manually in the settings or as an LFO or Mod Lane destination.

Amplitude is set by the default velocity or the actual, altered velocity value (for example after modulation). The decay curve is determined between max and the sustain level.

Modulating gate and velocity will also affect the decay and amplitude. This could be by using modulation lanes, LFOs, or randomization.

### T.Env Settings

Display:
```
CV 1 - SEQ 1
Type    Voice
T.Env   1       0       0%
                Attk    Sust
```

| Field | Function |
|---|---|
| Type — T.Env | Envelope is the output to control as defined by the available parameter settings. |
| Voice — Voice Index | Voice index based on sequencer mode. The envelope needs 1 voice. |
| Attk — Attack time | Time for the envelope to reach full value. Full value determined by the triggered note velocity. |
| Sust — Sustain level | Level that the envelope is held while the note is active. Set as a % of the amplitude. |

### LFO & MOD (CV Types)

The modulation functions of LFO and MOD operate on the CV outputs in a similar way. There are 2 LFOs and 8 modulation lanes which can be used to send modulation to CV outputs. Each would be selected individually. The grid is used to assign outputs to sequencers in CV/Gate view.

The LFO settings can be edited in the LFO pages. The parameters will affect the behaviour of the CV output, although the LFO parameter destination is independent and therefore is not affected by the CV routing set up in the CV/Gate page.

### Voltage range and offset

The CV output from LFO and MOD modulation is set to a 0 to 5V range, unipolar. The ONE hardware range is 8V, starting from -3V up to +5V. To get the output to operate over the full ± range, **Offset** should be adjusted. Setting an offset of -100 will operate over the -3V to +5V range.

| Offset | Output range |
|---|---|
| 0 | 0V to +5V (unipolar) |
| -100 | -3V to +5V (full bipolar) |

### LFO / MOD Settings

LFO display:
```
CV 1 - SEQ 1
Type    Offst
LFO 1   0       -       -
        -       -
```

MOD display:
```
CV 1 - SEQ 1
Type    Offst
MOD 1   0       -       -
        -       -
```

| Field | Function |
|---|---|
| Type — LFO 1/2 or MOD 1-8 | Selects an LFO or Modulation lane source to output to the CV port. |
| Offst — Offset | Adjust the voltage range of the modulation output. |

## 9.2 Gate Output Options

Gates are typically used to trigger an event, for example to trigger a note on. So for a Mono mode sequencer, 1 note can trigger 1 Gate and 1 CV to activate the note. For Chord mode (e.g. triggering a 3-note triad), the chord would need 1 Gate to trigger all of the notes and 3 x CVs to set the value of each note in the chord.

Gates can also be set to multiple voices if desired, limited by the sequencer mode.

As well as note triggers, the gates in ONE can be configured as a clock, accent or as an LFO threshold. Setting up gates follows the same process as for CVs: the grid is used to activate and deactivate the outputs, and the parameters can be set with the knobs and the display.

### Voltage range

The gate voltage output is typically 0-5V. This can also be set to 0-10V. The option to set this globally for all gate outputs is found in the configuration settings: **Config > Analog > Gate V Out**.

### Voice / Track selection

The voice can be selected on many of the options. For multitrack mode sequencers, a track can be selected as the alternative option to voice.

### Activating outputs

In the CV/Gate grid, tap a pad to activate or deactivate the CV or Gate output for sequencers 1-8. The display will indicate current setting and enable the parameters to be edited for the selected step.

Open the CV/Gate configuration menu by holding **[Shift] + press [Gen]**.

## Trig Type — Gate Output

Sets the gate output to operate as a trigger. Typically used for notes. Format set V-Trig, high represents a trigger. This is default. S-Trig when low is a trigger.

Gates can be configured to operate at 5V or 10V and has two operating formats which allows the output to be matched to one of the two main standards.

Turn (Type) Knob 1 to select the option.

### Trig Settings

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Type — Trig | Sets the output to operate as a trigger. Typically used for notes. |
| Format — V-Trig / S-Trig | Voltage format. |

### Trig Voltage Formats

| Format | Range |
|---|---|
| V-Trig | 0V to +5V — output is high when triggering, remains low when off. |
| S-Trig | +5V to 0V — output is low when triggering, remains high when off. Can also be used as an inverted gate. |

The trigger will remain active while a note is held.

Example display: `GATE 1 - SEQ 1   Type: Trig   Voice: 1   Format: V-Trig   Offst: -`

## Clock Type — Gate Output

Sets the gate output to operate as a clock. A pulse is output based on the defined rate configured in the settings. The rate value is independent from other sequencer rates.

Clock pulse has 50% duty cycle, duration of pulse based on the rate. So for example, with the default rate of 1/16th, each pulse width is 1/32nd duration.

The sequencer must be running for the clock output to be active.

Turn (Type) Knob 1 to select the option.

### Clock Settings

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Type — Clock | Sets the output to operate as a clock pulse. |
| Rate | Clock speed. Independent rate from sequencer, but set in time divisions. |

### Rate Options

Bar, 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, 1/8t, 1/16, 1/16t, 1/32, 1/32t.

Example display: `GATE 1 - SEQ 1   Type: Clock   Rate: 1/16`

## Accent Type — Gate Output

Accents allow a gate output to be configured to trigger when the velocity value exceeds a defined value. This allows destination parameters such as triggering hat or rim sound, sub bass or affecting a parameter.

The accent function monitors the velocity of the notes in a sequencer pattern. The track or voice can be assigned for the accent monitoring.

When a velocity value for a note in the sequencer pattern exceeds the threshold a gate is triggered. The trigger will stay high until the next trigger update. If the next trigger update is below the threshold, it will reset.

The output will only be triggered when the threshold of the velocity of a note in the current pattern is exceeded. This can be set per voice or aligned to a track in multi track mode.

Turn (Type) Knob 1 to select the option.

### Accent Settings

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Type — Accnt | Sets the output to operate as a trigger. Typically used for notes. |
| Voice / Track | Selects a voice or track to monitor. The velocity value is checked against threshold value. |
| Trhld | Sets the threshold value for the velocity check. When exceeded the gate will be triggered. |

### Velocity Range

| Range | Value |
|---|---|
| Threshold | 0 to 127 |

Example display: `GATE 1 - SEQ 1   Type: Accnt   Voice: 1   Trhld: 64`

Example step velocities triggering accent gate: steps with velocity 110 and 75 exceed a threshold of 40, producing accent on; step at 75 still on if threshold lower; threshold 64 turns accent off when step velocity falls below.

## Threshold Type — Gate Output

Threshold allows a gate output to be configured to trigger when the value of an LFO or Mod lane source exceeds a defined value. This allows destination functions to be triggered based on the modulation value, for example turning on a filter if modulation is above a certain level.

The threshold function monitors a selected LFO or Mod lane amount in a sequencer. The track or voice can be assigned for monitoring.

When an LFO or Mod amount in the sequencer exceeds the threshold a gate is triggered. The trigger will stay high until the value falls below the threshold and then it will reset.

The output will only be triggered when the threshold of the modulation in the pattern is exceeded. This can be set per voice or aligned to a track in multi track mode.

Turn (Type) Knob 1 to select the option.

### Threshold Settings

| Parameter | Description |
|---|---|
| Type — Thrsh | Sets the output to operate as a trigger. Typically used for notes. |
| Type (Modulation Source) | Selects an LFO or Mod lane as the modulation source to monitor the amount. |
| Trhld | Sets the threshold amount for the modulation check. When exceeded the gate will be triggered. |

### Modulation Range

| Range | Value |
|---|---|
| Modulation Amount | 0 to 100% |

Example display: `GATE 1 - SEQ 1   Type: Thrsh   Type: Lfo 1   Trhld: 64`

## 9.3 Analog Drum Application

This example application demonstrates a single voice using individual gate outputs. A simple example is to illustrate gate outputs to control a drum module. OXI ONE is coupled with a Eurorack drum module, Trinity from Modbap Modular.

### Configuration

| Element | Setting |
|---|---|
| Sequencer mode | Multitrack sequencer. Track 1, 2, 3 programmed with the drum patterns. |
| Rationale | A multitrack sequencer fits this application well as note is less important when only triggering a gate. Multitrack also has the drum grid when pressing [Keyboard]. |
| Track 1 destination | Kick |
| Track 2 destination | Perc |
| Track 3 destination | Hats |

### CV / Gate Outputs Available

| Output Pair | Output Pair |
|---|---|
| Cv 1 / Gate 1 | Cv 5 / Gate 5 |
| Cv 2 / Gate 2 | Cv 6 / Gate 6 |
| Cv 3 / Gate 3 | Cv 7 / Gate 7 |
| Cv 4 / Gate 4 | Cv 8 / Gate 8 |

## 9.4 Analog Synth Voice

This example application demonstrates a mono synth voice application using a CV and gate output. A simple example is to illustrate gate outputs to control OXI Coral in a Eurorack setup.

If a polyphonic voice is required, multiple CVs can be configured and one gate based on the rack setup to control. Coral has the unique ability to play polyphonically from one CV based on note values so one CV & Gate works well with Coral. Multiple oscillators or modules may be needed when using other setups for polyphonic voices.

This example can be followed for mono setups (or Poly with Coral).

### Configuration

| Element | Setting |
|---|---|
| Sequencer mode | Mono sequencer. Mono suits this application, but if multiple voices are needed for polyphonic outputs then a poly mode sequencer would be the best choice. |
| Output assignment | Seq 1 is set to Gate 1 and CV 1 Out. Configured as a mono mode sequencer. |
| Trig | Triggers to play the note in Coral as transmitted from the ONE gate output. The CV will represent pitch. |
| Oct | In Coral 'Oct' represents the 1V/Oct input typical for oscillators and synths in order to control pitch. Each CV will transmit a CV value calibrated for note pitch. |

Here we are using one voice and letting Coral do the poly work.

## 9.5 Analog Clock I/O

OXI ONE has both a clock input, for synchronising ONE to external gear, and a clock output where ONE can be the primary clock lead for other external gear. These I/O options follow the classic analog clock models, but ONE can also operate by using a MIDI clock.

Clk In is also available from CV Input 2 which is used as a Reset / Stop-Start input by using a TRS to TR-TR splitter and can be routed using the mod matrix. It is important to ensure that the CV In 2 is not used for modulation if it is used for synchronisation.

The clock timing in PPQN can be set in the config > analog settings. This must be matched up between devices.

### TRS Connector Pinout (Clock)

| Pin | Signal |
|---|---|
| Tip | Clock Signal |
| Ring | Start / Stop / Reset |
| Sleeve | Ground |

Stereo TRS handles both the clock and the reset, start & stop signals. These can be separated using a splitter cable to 2 x Mono inputs or outputs. This will allow the clock and reset to be patched independently. Also the splitter must be used for CV in 2.

### Clock Start / Stop / Reset Behaviour

The behavior of the clock input and output can be configured in the Config > Analog > Reset / Start - Stop (IN & OUT) Settings. Ensure this setting is 'OFF' if using the Clk input or CV In 2 for modulation.

| Config Setting | Input Behaviour | Output Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| Off | Reset is ignored. With OXI ONE 'Sync' set to Clk, the sequencer will start as soon as a clock signal is received. Does not use Start/Stop/Reset, typically mono TR patch cabled. | Reset signals deactivated. With OXI ONE 'Sync' set to Clk, the sequencer will start as soon as a clock signal is received. Does not use Start/Stop/Reset, typically mono TR patch cabled. |
| Pulse Reset | Input behaves as a reset signal. When OXI ONE 'Sync' is set to Clk and a reset pulse is received at the reset input, ONE will reset. Seq playheads are reset. | Output behaves as a reset signal. Short 4ms pulse is sent out as the reset signal just before the first clock signal when ONE starts or continues playing. |
| Run - Stop | When OXI ONE 'Sync' is set to Clk, ONE will only play if run-stop or start-stop is high. Otherwise, if low the sequencer is stopped. Some synths and gear expect this alternate reset operating model. | Run-stop or start-stop output signal is high when the sequencers are playing and low when stopped. Some synths and gear expect this alternate reset operating model. |

## 9.6 OXI Pipe MKII Connections

OXI Pipe MKII is a Eurorack module which works exclusively with ONE MKII. Pipe replicates all 8 CV and Gate outputs from those on the rear of ONE onto the Pipe module front panel.

Pipe connects to ONE using a supplied, single mini HDMI to standard HDMI cable. Pipe also splits the clock and reset inputs making these also available as individual inputs. Pipe can also be used to link two racks using a full size 2.0 HDMI or 4K cable.

### Pipe MKII Specifications

| Spec | Value |
|---|---|
| Size | 6HP Eurorack module |
| Power | Passive — needs no power |
| Connection to ONE | Mini HDMI to standard HDMI cable |
| Outputs replicated | All 8 CV / Gate outputs |
| Additional outputs | Clock Out, Reset Out (split from ONE inputs) |
| Inter-rack linking | Full size 2.0 HDMI or 4K cable between two Pipe modules |

### Cable Recommendation

It is recommended to keep the HDMI cable between ONE and the Pipe module or between two Pipe modules as short as possible in order to retain a high quality and reliable communication link. Also ensure the cable is 2.0 standard. Pipe is not compatible with other Eurorack devices or modules and is exclusively used with ONE.

## 9.7 MIDI Connectivity

OXI ONE can be used to control MIDI devices, both through note messages and by using CC and PC messages. MIDI can be connected using 3 methods.

- TRS MIDI in and out. Can also connect to classic MIDI 5-Pin DIN style connections with a Type 'A' adapter.
- MIDI over USB using the type C connector. Ideal for PC/Mac hosted DAWs or applications.
- MIDI over Bluetooth Low Energy (BLE) for wireless communications.

TRS MIDI Type 'A' is the OXI ONE standard.

### MIDI Thru Configuration

MIDI Thru can be set in the Config > MIDI settings for TRS, USB or BLE. If set to On, MIDI messages from this input will be echoed to the other MIDI outputs. If set to off MIDI will not be automatically passed through the device to an output.

### MIDI USB

When working with USB MIDI the system setting 'USB Mode' must be set appropriately for the application. The most common option is device mode but others are also available.

On the OXI ONE, USB Mode settings: Config > System > USB Mode.

### USB Modes

| Mode | Description |
|---|---|
| Device | OXI Acts as a USB MIDI Device, taking power through the USB interface. |
| Host + Power | OXI Acts as a USB MIDI Host, supplying power through the USB interface to other devices. May affect OXI ONE battery charge life due to additional power consumption. |
| Host No Power | OXI Acts as a USB MIDI Host, but without supplying power to other devices. Some USB adapters require this configuration to operate. |
| Device Self Powered | OXI Acts as a USB MIDI Device, without taking power through the USB interface. Self powered — better option to avoid ground loops and USB power noise. Disconnect and reconnect the USB cable when changing this setting. |

Device mode normally acts like a peripheral device where another item governs and controls the MIDI activity (e.g. a keyboard or mouse taking their lead from a PC which acts as the host). A host is the primary MIDI control where MIDI messages are interpreted and governs any other connected MIDI devices.

### Supported Host-Mode Devices (selected)

Roli Seaboard, Arturia Keystep, Akai LPK25 & MPD218, Korg NanoKONTROL2, ASM Hydrasynth, OXI ONE, Miditech Minicontrol32, TE TX-6, TE OP-1 Field, Roland MC101 & MC707, Moog One/16/5, Waldorf Streichfett & Blofeld, Novation 25 SL MkII, Launch Control XL, Launchpad Pro Mk3 & S, QuNexus, Conductive Labs NDLR, Elektron Syntakt.

### Example: USB MIDI OXI ONE to Mac/PC DAW

MIDI input can be selected in the PC/Mac DAW to control the virtual instruments and plug ins. MIDI can be connected directly using the USB-C connection on OXI ONE and the USB on the target PC/Mac. OXI ONE would be set as a 'device' in this mode and can be charged by the PC/Mac power supply.

### USB Performance Solutions

USB can be used to charge / power as well as carry digital data in and out of the devices. It is essential to use a compatible cable for MIDI. Some USB cables are designed for power only and can be problematic. Ensure the cable is fully data compliant.

### OXI ONE in Device Mode

Use a USB Isolator with external supply input to avoid supply/ground loop noises. The isolator requires an external power source, such as a wall charger. Without this, the power supplied to the OXI ONE will be insufficient, resulting in a gradual depletion of its charge.

Alternatively to avoid grounding noise you can set the OXI ONE in 'Device Self Powered' mode. This may not work with some hosts.

### OXI ONE in Host Mode

Tested setups:

- Some devices like the Roli Seaboard work with a USB C to C cable. No need for any adaptor.
- A simple USB C to A adaptor is another great solution to use the OXI ONE as a USB Host and power supply. The OXI ONE has to be set to 'Host + Power' in this case. Note the power and battery limitations when connecting 'hungry' devices.
- USB C Power splitter. Use this kind of adaptor when you want to power the OXI ONE and have a USB MIDI device connected at the same time. The OXI ONE should be set to 'Host No Power'. The USB C male port should be connected to the OXI ONE which is the Host, the MIDI device should be connected to the USB A female plug (or USB C in some splitters, identified with a 'data' symbol instead of power) and the USB C female plug is the power input. Example brands: BCLink, Ruxely, Moswag, Mogood.

### MIDI Bluetooth (BLE)

OXI ONE uses BLE MIDI or Bluetooth Low Energy for wireless communications. BLE can sometimes be technically challenging to set up on some devices so it is advised to use a MIDI dongle such as WIDI Master, WIDI Jack or WIDI uHost when using devices like iPads. Android can be more robust for MIDI BLE.

OXI ONE is a dual role device and can operate with BLE as:

- **Central Role** — OXI ONE acts as a central hub for Bluetooth MIDI when connecting peripherals such as keyboards (e.g. ROLI Seaboard, CME Xkey Air), BLE Dongles (e.g. CME WIDI BUD), BLE MIDI adapters (e.g. WIDI Jack), controllers (e.g. Korg Nanokey, Akai LPD8) or devices like the Teenage Engineering OP-Z. ONE will automatically look for a peripheral device to pair with directly. Only one device connection is supported.
- **Peripheral Role** — Connects to a central device such as an iOS or Android device, PC, Mac, and devices like CME WIDI Master. Ensure the external device has Bluetooth turned on in order to pair. The pairing process will be determined by the device itself and may differ between devices.

### Enabling Bluetooth

On the OXI ONE, turn Bluetooth on in: Config > System > Bluetooth On/Off. Once the option is selected, tap knob to confirm selection and make the Bluetooth connection active or inactive.

### Example: MIDI Bluetooth OXI ONE to iPad AUM App

- Config > System > Bluetooth On/Off = ON
- In AUM: Wireless MIDI Bluetooth Central = OXI ONE MKII BLE
- iPad is the peripheral device
- In the AUM MIDI Routing matrix connect between OXI ONE BLE and the loaded device in the track

## 9.8 MIDI Ports & Channels

OXI ONE has the ability to interface with multiple MIDI ports, each with 16 MIDI channels. Port A and B are the default ports integral to the ONE device. These may be identified as 1 and 2 on some external DAWs and devices.

The 16 channels in ONE are assigned per sequencer, using the [Shift] + Seq [1] - [8] settings menu. The first page allows channels to be assigned by sequencer or by track for a multitrack sequencer.

The MIDI Channel is set per sequencer or per track. Options are 1-16, for each port A, B etc. Turn (Type) Knob 1 to select the option.

Example display: `+ SEQ1 Setup 1/3   Ch: 1 A   PC: OFF   Out: ALL   Mode: MONO`

### Expansion via OXI Split

The ports can be expanded from the defaults by using OXI Split. This is a low cost option that increases the channel count to 96 by expanding the ports. Split is designed exclusively for expanding OXI ONE and is not generically compatible with other MIDI devices.

Incoming MIDI to ONE can be passed thru to the other MIDI output channels using Split. To work, OXI Split II must be configured in: Config > MIDI > OXI Split selection. This can be selected to the original OXI Split V1 (3 outs) or to OXI Split V2 (6 outs). This choice depends on the OXI Split version connected. If Split is removed, the setting must be restored to 'OFF' to use the standard MIDI ports.

OXI Split is connected using a TRS cable between ONE MIDI Out and Split In port. Other devices can then be connected using the 2 × 5-Pin DIN style outputs or the 4 × TRS MIDI Outputs. MIDI out or thru is selectable in ONE while the TRS type A or B is selectable on the Split device for each of the TRS outputs. Each output has 16 channels.

### OXI Split Port / Channel Map

| Port | Channels | DAW Identifier |
|---|---|---|
| Port A | 1-16 | A / 1 |
| Port B | 1-16 | B / 2 |
| Port C | 1-16 | C / 3 |
| Port D | 1-16 | D / 4 |
| Port E | 1-16 | E / 5 |
| Port F | 1-16 | F / 6 |
| Thru | — | — |

## 9.9 Synchronization

Synchronization refers to the alignment of transport control and especially the clock timing between devices. The assignment of a device as a master clock would set the tempo as a primary lead for other secondary devices to follow. The default on start up is for sequencer 1 to be selected and the OXI ONE synchronization set to 'Internal'. This can be changed depending on the application and setup required.

Hold [Shift] + tap [Stop] to alter the sync setting while the sequencer is in a stopped state. The synchronization option is displayed in the page header.

MIDI clock timing is sent and recognised as 24 PPQN (pulses per quarter note) in OXI ONE. This is standard for MIDI Clocks and will synchronize easily with most MIDI devices. Analog clock timing can be adjusted in the OXI ONE config menu.

### Sync Options

Using [Shift] + [Stop] while the sequencer is stopped, the following sync options are available:

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| INT | OXI ONE uses its own internal clock. ONE can act as a primary lead in this situation, sending its clock for other devices to follow and the Tempo BPM is controlled in ONE. |
| USB | OXI ONE uses an external clock received on the USB MIDI port. ONE acts as a secondary follower, following the clock from another device. Tempo BPM is controlled externally and cannot be adjusted in ONE. |
| MIDI | OXI ONE uses an external clock received on the TRS MIDI input port or by MIDI Bluetooth. ONE acts as a secondary follower. Tempo BPM is controlled externally and cannot be adjusted in ONE. |
| CLK | OXI ONE uses an external clock received on the analog CLK input port. ONE acts as a secondary follower. Tempo BPM is controlled externally and cannot be adjusted in ONE. |

### Latency & DAWs

Latency is the delay which can occur when communicating between connected devices. This is not normally a noticeable issue with hardware devices but with PC/Mac based DAWs it can be a problem. For this reason most DAWs have a compensation option for clocks. If ONE or the DAW acts as a master clock it is worth checking latency and adjusting the delay compensation. In Ableton Live this is called 'MIDI Clock Sync Delay' and is found in the DAW's MIDI settings.

### Auto Sync

Auto sync allows ONE to respond to incoming transport messages. Auto sync is set in the Config > Performance menu.

| Option | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| ON | Auto sync is enabled. When ONE is stopped it will respond to a MIDI Start message received from an external device by automatically playing. Applies to incoming messages via MIDI TRS, MIDI USB, or MIDI Bluetooth. The Analog CLK input can also trigger a start. The external clock will control tempo. |
| OFF | Auto sync is disabled. ONE will not respond to any incoming transport start message. |

### Internal Clock Fallback

If a MIDI stop message is received or the external synchronization source is lost, ONE will revert to internal synchronization. This only applies to the MIDI clock and not to the analog CLK input.

OXI ONE has two analog clock ports, one input and one output. Typically these are used with modular gear. The behavior of these is controlled with the Start-Stop/Reset options found in the config menu.

## 9.10 MIDI Input Monitor

MIDI Monitor is a utility used for checking incoming MIDI activity within OXI ONE from all routes and channels. It is a simple, yet highly useful tool available when needed from the config menu or directly on the user panel.

### Enabling

- Turn MIDI Monitor on in: Config > System > MIDI IN Monitor.
- Hold [Shift] + hold [Back] > 2 seconds to open the MIDI Monitor.
- Alternatively access via the config menu.

### Display

The last 3 messages are displayed. If a clock is monitored this may overwrite any notes or CC information. Shows Connection, Channel and message.

Example (Note numbers + velocity, note 'on' message):

```
MIDI IN Monitor
USB Ch 1: N.Off 64
USB Ch 1: N.On  65 110
USB Ch 1: N.Off 65
```

## 9.11 Instrument Definitions

An instrument definition is a predefined configuration for MIDI CC mapping to external devices. Instead of manually setting up each CC to control an external device parameter, the instrument definition for that selected equipment will map the parameters automatically and apply their names. This saves time and is an efficient way to work with MIDI continuous control messages for external gear.

### Selection

The instrument definition for a device is selected in the main sequencer settings, page 3, parameter 'Instr' for the sequencer.

Example display: `SEQ1 Setup 3/3   Instr: AdMc   Color: 79`

Example: Audiothingies MicroMonsta2 connected by MIDI — CC control can be performed from ONE without manual configuration once the AdMc instrument definition is selected.

### Management

The OXI App can be used to transfer instrument definition files between OXI ONE and a PC/Mac. Instrument definitions are not user configurable on the device but can be created in the OXI App. These can be created individually and then loaded to OXI ONE using the app.

If the manufacturer changes the CC mapping of the device the instrument definition may not work as originally configured. Instrument definitions are saved with the pattern and can be backed up with the app.
